- In one line, as the manual will say it: the service answers the register's text at `n`, and the reader decides (svc:286; Y:4093–4100).
- What it fixes: where the socket lives and how both ends check it, who may connect, what a request carries, the three answers, the limits, how the service connects, how it starts and stops, `serve --start`, and the client's side as far as the socket reaches. Section 13 gives the bytes on the socket, lane 5's proposal with lane 1's U5 bytes in place of lane 5's wherever lane 1 gave them; section 14 lists each draft's changes, and section 15 lists the open points, each closed with its ruling or answer, or still open with its owners. Nothing here adds a value the sources do not give, except section 13's proposals, each marked "(proposal, lane 5)" with its reason, lane 1's bytes, each marked with its item of lane 1's U5 review or of its U5 re-check, and the two sidecar files' bytes, which sections 2.4 and 8.6 take from T512's code as built, with its lines; and the details of lane 3's rulings OP-18 (b) and OP-19 (b), which lane 5 settles, each marked "lane 5's details" (sections 2.4 and 8.4).

- **#165** is opensoft/openRepoTools#165, and a number after it is a comment id.
- **Names.** The service is `lanes-index serve`; the documentation and its log call it `lanes-register` (Y:3835–3842). Its client is `lanes-index register read` (Y:3843–3848). "The client" below means `register read`'s socket path, and "the guard" means `lanes-edit.sh guard`.

## 1. What the socket serves, and to whom

- **One service per environment,** a bench container or a host shell (Y:3701; tasks:1390). It connects as its engineer's reader login, `lanes_r_<engineer>`, a member of READER, which the environment's `lanes-index.conf` names (Y:3708–3713).
- **What it answers:** the register's text at the journal's head `n`, read fresh for each request. The reader runs today's parser on that text in its own process, and decides (svc:10; Y:4093–4100). The service answers no question by key (Y:4093–4095).
- **Its clients** are `register read`'s socket path, "every reader's path" (Y:3843–3845), and the guard's prefetch, `register read --for guard --prefetch` (Y:3845–3848, Y:4127–4133). In lane 1's terms these are forms 1 and 2 of `register read` (CLI44:655–660), and form 3, the readers' second read, which asks the service first too (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-7; CLI47:730; section 13.9).
- **Not its clients:**
  - **form 4,** the guard's second read: it "always reads directly, never through the service" (B7, its :12; CLI44:747; research.md ERR-04; tasks:1384);
  - **the `SessionStart` block,** which "only starts the service and asks it nothing" (Y:3828–3829; svc:93; Y:4493–4499). Its start is `serve --start` (section 9);
  - **every writer:** "No writer is a client (4A): a write's snapshot and commit open their own connections with the REGISTER-WRITER credential, and never wait for or start the service" (Y:3829–3831; svc:10).
- **Only on `mode=primary`.** Clients ask the socket only on a primary workstation, and in files and `index` mode the guard and the readers "stay exactly as today" (Y:3948–3951). Section 8.5 says when `serve` runs at all.

## 2. The socket's place, and its checks

### 2.1 The path

- **One path, no choice:** `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/openRepoTools/run/register.sock`, in `run/` under `lanes-index`'s state directory (Y:3753–3755, Y:3767; tasks:1391). `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` is not consulted (Y:3755–3757).
- **A process with another `XDG_STATE_HOME`** finds no service, starts its own at its own path, and is answered correctly by it, at the cost of a second connection (Y:3757–3760).
- **Not `/tmp`:** a name there can be taken first by another user, and `tmp` reapers delete sockets in it (Y:3771–3773).
- **The length.** The client checks the path's length before it connects or starts anything. Over 103 bytes it reads directly and starts nothing, and `lanes-index serve` refuses the same path with exit 2 and the stamp `path-too-long` (Y:3777–3780, Y:3927). "Nothing shortens a path silently" (Y:3780–3781). 103 bytes is macOS's limit; Linux's is 107 (Y:3774–3776).
  - **Bytes, as the file system takes them.** The length is the path's encoded length, `len(os.fsencode(path))`, with no terminating NUL counted, never its count of characters, so a state directory whose name holds multibyte characters cannot pass the check and then fail at `bind()`. Both ends measure it by one function (`lanes-index`:7568–7572, used at :7758, :7820 and :9082 at line R's merge106; the Codex review, SHOULD 6).

### 2.2 The directory: 0700, this uid, never a symlink

- **Checked on every use,** by the service before it binds and by the client before it connects or starts one: it must be a directory, not a symlink (`lstat`), owned by this uid, with mode 0700 (Y:3761–3763; tasks:1391).
- **Made so when absent:** `lanes-index` creates it that way (Y:3763–3764).
- **Never used, never repaired.** A directory that fails the check is not used: "the client reads directly, the service exits 2 naming it, and `lanes-index status` reports it" (Y:3764–3766).
- **Never stamped.** `dir-check` is never stamped: `serve` exits 2 naming the directory (Y:3765) and writes nothing in it, and `lanes-index status` reports the failure itself (Y:3761–3768). That is lane 3's ruling (#165 6746215510, OP-9), with an erratum on Y:3927–3929, whose stamped causes name `dir-check`: ERR-28. Lane 1 reviews the `status` line (R44). Section 8.6 follows it.

### 2.3 The files

- **Five files,** all in `run/`: `register.sock`, `register.lock`, `register.owner`, `register.failed` and `register.log` (Y:3767–3768).
- **The bind:** the service binds under `umask 077`, and the directory's check is what stops another user (Y:3768–3770).
- **Nothing secret in them.** `register.log` and every `refuse db <cause>` carry fixed phrases and the SQLSTATE only, never `psql`'s message text and never a URL (Y:3797–3799). The one label the service may name is `where`, with any userinfo removed (Y:3816–3818).

### 2.4 The owner file, `register.owner`: install, namespace and boot

- **It holds** the service's pid, its start UTC, its install's identity, and on Linux its pid namespace (`readlink /proc/self/ns/pid`) and the boot id (`/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`) (Y:3782–3784). The install's identity is the size, mtime and inode of the service's own `lanes-index` file (Y:3915–3916), and with T124 its module's (below).
- **Another namespace or boot.** A client whose own pid namespace or `boot_id` differs treats the service as unavailable (Y:3784–3786; tasks:1393): it reads directly and starts none, so SVC-1 holds where a bench bind-mounts `~/.local/state` across containers (Y:3786–3788). That is SVC-2's text, and lane 3's ruling (#165 6746655077, OP-8, which corrects 6746215510's OP-8 line).
  - **`ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED` still starts one,** whatever the owner file says (6746655077). So the owner file's namespace and boot decide only for a socket that answers the connect (section 13.8).
  - **In B4:** an erratum, ERR-27, drops "another namespace or" from Y:3882–3883's start list, which keeps "an owner file from another install, unless a failure stamp is fresh"; and tasks:1393's "as absent" (B3's `tasks.md:1407`) becomes "as unavailable, and starts none", for another namespace or boot (6746655077).
- **Another install.** A client that finds an owner file from another install starts a service (Y:3881–3883). The old service answers its next request `unavailable restarting`, and exits, where its own files changed (Y:3915–3918). Lane 3's correction keeps this case as it stands (#165 6746655077, OP-8). An old service whose own files did not change does not, a v1.3 limit: a new install reached by repointing a link above the path the old service was started by, or any second live install at another real path, such as a checkout run by hand (section 8.4; OP-20; lane 1's U5 NIT 3, #165 6895008151; lane 3's ruling, #165 6895062857).
- **On macOS** the owner file holds no namespace or boot id: Y gives those "on Linux" (Y:3783).
- **Its bytes, as T512 writes them** (the Codex review, BLOCKING 4; `lanes-index`:8042–8075 at line R's merge106). UTF-8 text, one `key=value` line for each fact, each line ending in one `\n`, in this order:
  - `pid=<pid>`, in decimal;
  - `started=<UTC>`, as `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, in whole seconds;
  - `install=<size> <mtime> <inode>`, or `install=<size> <mtime> <inode> <size> <mtime> <inode>`: first the size in bytes, the mtime in whole nanoseconds and the inode, as `os.stat` gives them, of the service's own `lanes-index` file by its real path, `os.path.realpath(__file__)`, taken once, which is T124's launcher once T124 has moved the body out; then, where `lanes_index_main.py` is in the directory of that real path, as T124's launcher finds it, the same three of that module; each in decimal, with one space between. With no module file, as in every tree before T124, it is the first three alone. Nothing after the `=` where the service could not `stat` either file for any reason but the module's absence, which makes the file unusable to every client (below). The six, and the module's absence as three, are follow-up 1's part 2, lane 5's choice (a) (#165 6890303231), which lane 3's ruling takes (#165 6894116246), as built in T512's `7dd91ccd` (`REGISTER_SERVE_MODULE` and `register_serve_install`, `lanes-index`:8113–8148, and `register_serve_owner_text`, :8167–8174, at `7dd91ccd`). The launcher's `stat` by its real path is follow-up 1's part 4, as built in T512's `23cb3799` (`register_serve_install`, `lanes-index`:8116–8151 at `23cb3799`, :8153–8187 at `4e343ea2`), where `7dd91ccd` took the launcher's three numbers by `os.path.abspath(__file__)`, a lexical join, by which `a/link/../lanes-index` names another launcher's file, or none (lane 1's R2 review, NIT 1, #165 6892234131); and the service is started by the launcher's path as invoked, made absolute with no lexical normalization, which names the same file, as built in T512's `4e343ea2` (section 8.2). Its landing order, part 3, as lane 3's ruling gives it in part 4: a client from before (a) reads a six-number owner file as unusable, and starts none, so the six-number form, T512's `7dd91ccd`, lands with or before T512's first release, and no released client meets a six-number file it cannot use (lane 1's R2 review of `7dd91ccd`, NIT 4, #165 6892234131); and "T124 lands only together with T512's `7dd91ccd` and `23cb3799` and lane 2's T237zzf, never with one of them alone" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6894116246), T237zzf being the guard module's `_owner_lines`, since without them the guard's own test estate writes three numbers where the client gives six, and its cases start a real service (lane 1's run r4), while T237zzf without `7dd91ccd` gives a six-number file the client cannot use (lane 1's R2 review, SHOULD 1, #165 6892234131);
  - on Linux, `boot=<boot id>`, the boot id file's text with its newline stripped, and `pidns=<link>`, the text `readlink /proc/self/ns/pid` gives, such as `pid:[4026531836]`, each only where it could be read.
- **No version line.** A reader takes the keys it knows and passes over every other line, so a later format may add a key, and never changes what a key means. Both ends are one install in any case, since a client that finds another install's file starts a service (above; section 13.3's reason for `read 1`).
- **How it is written** (`lanes-index`:7575–7589 and :8843–8861 at line R's merge106): whole, mode 0600, by a temporary file beside it and `rename`, so a reader finds the old file or the new, never part of one. The service writes it under `register.lock`, after it binds and listens at a name of its own in `run/`, and before it renames that socket onto `register.sock`. So a client whose connect to the fixed path succeeds finds the file of the service it reached, unless its read races the next service's `rename`, which can show it that service's file, or none it can use, while it holds the old service's socket ("A read that races a restart", below; the Codex delta review, the NIT at draft 13's line 142); and a service that cannot write the file exits 69 before its socket appears. A temporary socket that a `SIGKILL` leaves between the bind and the `rename` is removed by the next start: the starter that holds `register.lock` and has found no live service removes, just before its own bind, each socket in `run/` of this uid whose name is `s.` and four lower-case hex digits, checked by `lstat`, logs one it cannot remove, and leaves it, and leaves any other name, or any file of that name that is not this uid's socket. Each such socket is a leftover, since only the lock's holder binds, and it renames or removes its temporary socket before it lets the lock go (lane 1's N5, #165 6881993079; `register_serve_sweep` and its pattern, `lanes-index`:9170 and :9177–9201, called at :9262, before the start draws its own name at :9263 and binds it, at T512's `bb19b1ad`, its commit `a447ef6a`). The start's bind: it draws a temporary name, and where the bind finds that name taken, `EADDRINUSE`, by an entry it did not make, whatever the entry is, it leaves the entry, logs the name, and draws another, up to 8 names, after which it says that the socket "could not be bound" and exits 69, as before; and it unlinks a temporary name only where this attempt's own `bind` made it and has not yet renamed it. As built at T512's `1e1846e3`, its round 5 (commits `6cc950f8` and `30ee202f`): the sweep passes over a leftover gone before it is reached, `ENOENT`, and writes any other failure, to list `run/` or to `lstat` or remove a leftover, to `register.log` (`register_serve_sweep`, `lanes-index`:9259–9296); the names, `REGISTER_SERVE_BIND_TRIES`, 8 (:9236), drawn at :9377–9403; and the unlink, :9392–9396. At `bb19b1ad` the sweep passed over every failure unsaid, so a leftover it could not remove stayed, and a start that drew its name exited 69; and the start's error path unlinked the name it drew, whether or not it had made it (Codex's review of T512 at `bb19b1ad`, SHOULD 3 and NIT 6; `lanes-index`:9188–9201, :9262–9279 and :11412 there, the exit's return at :9279 and its value, 69, at :11412; lane 3's Codex delta reviews of drafts 18 and 19, the bind-error cite, a NIT). Draft 14 said such a socket was "never removed", which was not exact: at `f21ca4a7`, it lasted until a start drew the same name, and that start's bind failed, so it exited 69 with no stamp, and its error path unlinked the leftover (`lanes-index`:9148–9164 at `f21ca4a7`). Nothing removes the file: the next service replaces it, and a stale one is read only behind a socket that answers, since a dead socket gives `ECONNREFUSED` first (section 2.5; 13.8).
- **How a client reads it** (the Codex re-review, B1, its order as built at T512's `88b50886`, `lanes-index`:8997–9010 and :9156–9173; S1, and OP-19 (b): lane 3's ruling, #165 6880543448, with lane 5's details; as built at T512's `26c77534`, `register_serve_owner_read`, `lanes-index`:9232–9259, and its caller, :9409–9439): only after its connect to the fixed path succeeds, and before it checks the server's uid or sends a byte (13.8). It reads at most 4,097 bytes, and only from a regular file: it opens the file with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK`, and reads it only where `fstat` finds a regular file, so a symbolic link or a FIFO there is a file it cannot read, and never blocks the client before its uid check (lane 1's N6, #165 6881993079; `_register_serve_read_regular`, :7625–7643 at `26c77534`). Where 4,097 bytes come back, the file is longer than 4,096 bytes, and unusable (below); otherwise the bytes read are the whole file, to its end, so their last byte is the physical file's. It decodes them as UTF-8 and splits them into lines as Python's `str.splitlines()` does; a line's key is the text before its first `=`, a line with no `=` is passed over, and so is a line whose key is none of the five known keys, `pid`, `started`, `install`, `pidns` and `boot`, however often that key appears. Then, in this order:
  - **A file it cannot use is unavailable** (OP-17, closed on (b): lane 3's ruling, #165 6879724890, point 1; OP-19, closed on (b): lane 3's ruling, #165 6880543448, with lane 5's details): a file that is absent, cannot be read, or is not a regular file; one longer than 4,096 bytes; one whose bytes are not UTF-8; one whose last byte is not `\n` (0x0A), an empty file among them; one with a known key on two lines; one with no `install=` line, or whose `install=` value is not exactly three runs of ASCII digits, or six, with one ASCII space between each, `[0-9]+ [0-9]+ [0-9]+(?: [0-9]+ [0-9]+ [0-9]+)?`, applied with `fullmatch` to the whole value, with no other character before, between or after them, and no cap on a run's digits, since an mtime in nanoseconds has 19 (the six: follow-up 1's part 2, by lane 3's ruling of (a), rule 4 accepting "three or six runs of ASCII digits, one space between each, and nothing else", #165 6894116246; `_REGISTER_SERVE_INSTALL_RE`, `lanes-index`:9534, applied at :9727–9729, at `7dd91ccd`); or, where the client has its own pid namespace or boot id, a file with no `pidns=` or no `boot=` line. The client closes the socket, starts none, writes `.direct`, and reads directly, and under `--for guard` form 2 prints the fallback line once (sections 11.2 and 11.3).
  - **Another namespace or boot is unavailable,** with the same outcome: where the client has its own pid namespace or boot id, a file whose `pidns=` or `boot=` differs from it, as text (above). Where the client cannot read its own, as on macOS or where `/proc` cannot be read, that comparison is skipped, and so is that line's requirement.
  - **Another install starts one:** a file whose `install=` differs from the client's own `lanes-index` file's, as text, is another install. With follow-up 1's part 2, which lane 3's ruling takes, the client's own value is its three numbers, or six with T124's module, and the test is unchanged, so a three-run file read by a client of six, a service started before T124 and a client after it, is another install (`lanes-index`:9735 at `7dd91ccd`), and the client starts a service, whose `rename` replaces the socket. Where the uid check that follows passes (next sub-bullet), the client starts a service and still sends its request, which the old service answers `unavailable restarting` where its own files changed (section 8.4's limit; OP-20) (above; section 8.1).
  - **Then the uid, which wins over another install** (lane 1's U5 NIT-h, #165 6881937840; lane 5's ruling, as T511's author, #165 6881986067): a server of another uid is unavailable (section 3), and the client sends nothing, starts no service and reads directly, whatever the owner file said of the install. As built at T512's `26c77534` (`lanes-index`:9434–9439, its commit `63f25fe9`); at `88b50886` the client kept another install's start there (:9177–9178).
- **A read that races a restart** (the Codex re-review, B1). The file is read only after the connect, so no file, stale or missing, stands between `ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED` and the start those call for (above; 13.8). Where the read races the next service's `rename` and finds the file absent, unreadable or unusable, the outcome is the first sub-bullet's: unavailable, with no start, and no second read against another service's file. Where it finds the next service's whole file while it holds the old service's socket, its request fails as `unavailable` when that socket ends. Either way the race costs a direct read and never a wrong answer.
- **Values in a file the client can use** (the Codex re-review, S1; OP-19, closed on (b): lane 3's ruling, #165 6880543448, with lane 5's details). Each known key comes at most once; `install=` has the form above; `pid=` and `started=` are read by `lanes-index status` alone, and are not checked beyond coming once; and `pidns=` and `boot=` are compared as text, so a malformed one is another namespace or boot, unavailable with no start. Lane 3's reason: the owner file decides where the client sends, so a torn or doubled file must not read as a good one. As built at T512's `26c77534` (`register_serve_owner_read`, `lanes-index`:9232–9259, with the known keys at :9225; `install=`'s form, :9229, checked by its caller at :9422–9424; its commit `595ccae4`). At `88b50886` the client read the first 4,096 bytes, took a key given twice at its last value, counted a last line with no `\n` as a line, and compared any `install=` as text, so a malformed one read as another install and started a service (:8997–9010 and :9160–9173). `lanes-index status` names the service's pid and start only from a file `register_serve_owner_read` takes, and otherwise says that "its owner file cannot be read" (`register_serve_state`, :9200–9221 at `26c77534`).
- **A service that cannot `stat` its own file** (OP-19 (b); lane 5's details, rule 7). Where the service cannot `stat` its own `lanes-index` file, or, with follow-up 1's part 2, which lane 3's ruling takes, T124's module beside it for any reason but the module's absence (`lanes-index`:8136–8147 at `7dd91ccd`), the line it writes is `install=` with nothing after the `=` (above; `register_serve_install` and `register_serve_owner_text`, `lanes-index`:8064–8071 and :8090–8097 at T512's `88b50886`), which can never have the form above. So that file is unusable by rule: every client reads directly and starts none, and the service stops at its idle stop, as section 8.4's "A service its clients cannot use stops this way" says. The next client then meets `ENOENT` and starts its own. There is no new exit code and no new `register.failed` cause. The writer is unchanged at T512's `26c77534` (`register_serve_install` and `register_serve_owner_text`, :8109–8116 and :8135–8142), and round 3's proof, on a scratch branch of its own, pins that the line `install=` never passes (`test_t512r3_op19_the_writers_failure_path_never_passes`). The same holds for a `lanes-index` file whose mtime is before 1970: its `install=` carries a negative mtime, which the form refuses, so every client reads directly.

### 2.5 A stale socket

- **What leaves one:** a service killed by `SIGKILL` leaves its socket file, and a container restart leaves the same stale file on the container's overlay (Y:3907, Y:3910–3911).
- **How it is cleared:** the next client gets `ECONNREFUSED` and starts a service, which takes `register.lock` and, under the lock, replaces the stale file (Y:3901–3903, Y:3907–3909; tasks:1398). Y has the starter unlink it and bind; as built, the starter binds and listens at a temporary name of its own, writes the owner file, and renames that socket over the stale one, as section 2.4's "How it is written" gives it (section 8.3; `lanes-index`:9263–9271 at T512's `bb19b1ad`; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, its fourth finding, a NIT, #165 6885771975).

## 3. Who may connect: this uid only

- **The service checks each client.** It reads `SO_PEERCRED` on each connection and closes any whose uid is not its own (Y:3789–3790). A client of another uid is answered nothing.
- **The client checks the service.** It reads `SO_PEERCRED` on its connected socket and treats a server of another uid as unavailable, "so a socket planted in a directory that somehow passed the check still answers nothing" (Y:3792–3794).
- **On macOS,** `LOCAL_PEERCRED` takes `SO_PEERCRED`'s place at both ends (tasks:1392). Y calls it "untested here", and where neither is available, "the directory's owner and mode are the whole protection" (Y:3794–3796). See OP-12, closed: T512 records it as not shown, with Y's fallback (lane 3's ruling, #165 6754633102).
- **Not yet shown:** no second uid was available to show the service's refusal (Y:3790–3792). Lane 2's T237 writes `test_a_client_of_another_uid_is_closed_by_so_peercred`, which replaces the peer-credential read in process, so it shows the refusal's logic on Linux, not a real second uid's (lane 2, #165 6754620468). The refusal itself stays not shown (Y:3790–3792).
- **Trust rests here, not on the digests.** The digests in an answer prove only that the bytes arrived as sent, "not a wrong service. Trust in the service rests on SVC-2: the checked directory, the owner file and the uid check at both ends" (Y:4070–4076).

## 4. The request: file names only

- **It names the files the client wants, and nothing else** (Y:4036–4037; svc:59; tasks:1416): `lanes/LANES.md`, `lanes/aliases.tsv`, `lanes/log/<lane>.md`, "and the rest of the render set" (Y:4036–4037). ERR-31 reads Y:4036–4037's "The request names the files it wants" as "the files it wants, singly or by the contract's member sets, or the render set's listing at `n`", and still nothing else: register files at `n`, and no pid, session or host fact (SVC-11). That is lane 3's ruling (#165 6747048305), which B5 records.
- **The guard's request** names `lanes/LANES.md`, `lanes/aliases.tsv` and `lanes/log/<window name>.md`, the log of the lane the window is normally bound to (Y:4142–4144), the last with `--lane=`, and only for a name in the class (CLI46:666, CLI46:670; Y:4956; lane 1's fix, #165 6746620707).
- **It never carries** a session id, `cwd`, agent id, prompt, window, live record or liveness fact (svc:59, svc:61–63; tp:2045–2047).
- **It never carries `n`,** nor a generation: "the request carries file names only, ... So it cannot be asked for a log at an older `n`, and form 4 never asks it" (tasks:1416; B7). So the guard's second read, at the `n` it prefetched, always reads directly and never asks the socket (B7; CLI44:747), and never reads `.direct` (B7; CLI44:688; lane 1's fix, #165 6746620707). svc:59 stands.
- **B7's condition stands with it:** "if T511's and T512's measurements show that path often running out of budget, lane 5 brings the point back; a request naming `n` would be a spec change, for Brett" (tasks:1384; B7). This contract measures nothing; T512's tests do.
- **The request's bytes** are section 13.3's: lane 5's proposal, with lane 1's U5 bytes in place of lane 5's (OP-3).
- **Sets, by the names they cover.** Form 1's members that the client cannot name before the read go in the same request by the render-set names they cover, file names only: `archive` as the set `lanes/archive/`; `--all`'s logs as the set `lanes/log/`, which the client filters to `check_lane_name`'s class; and `paths` as the render set's listing at `n`. The service resolves a set in the request's own transaction at `n`, and a set names files only, never a host-local fact or an `n` (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-5; CLI47:665; Y:4036–4041; Y:4611; B7). Section 13 encodes them in lane 1's bytes (lane 1's U5 review, M1).
- **The spec question is closed.** Lane 3 has ruled that a reader's request may name the files it wants singly or by the contract's member sets, or ask for the render set's listing at `n` (#165 6747048305), and B5 records it as ERR-31, an erratum on Y:4036–4037, the lines as lane 1 corrected their cite (#165 6747151224). Contract draft 4.7 states it (CLI47:665). So form 1's and form 3's sets go through the socket (13.9).
- **A log named in another case** is resolved by the service, case-insensitively among the render set's paths at its `n`, by T113's one function, which `serve` reuses (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-6; CLI46:673; SVC-3). That lookup compares bytes and folds A–Z only, for every key, in the class or outside it (lane 1's U5 review, 1(a)). Section 13.4 gives its records.

## 5. The answers

The service gives one of three answers to a request, and the client turns each into an exit (section 5.4). "`unavailable` never leaves the client" (Y:4336–4337).

### 5.1 The text at `n`, with digests, `n` and the generation

- **One snapshot per request.** For each request the service runs one transaction at REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY. It reads the journal's head `n`, the requested files' digests at `n`, and, in the same transaction, any journal entries or whole files it needs (Y:4036–4041; tasks:1410), and the members of any set the request names (CLI47:665). Like every form's snapshot, it reads the switch and checks `REGISTER_SCHEMA_VERSION` against `readers_from` and `version`, as a reader's first SELECT does, in the request's own transaction, never from a cached answer, and with T113's function (Y:2334, Y:2336; CLI46:653; lane 1's U5 review, M2): a switch not `primary` answers `refuse db switch-files -`, and a number outside the window answers `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>` (section 5.2). Its statements, from `BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` to `COMMIT`, are joined with psql's `\;` and sent as one query, so a request is one round trip (Y:3985–3988).
- **The generation, from the same snapshot.** The answer carries the register generation that transaction read, beside `n`. Y's interfaces table gives `register read` "text at `n` with digests, and the `n` and the register `generation` it read, in the same snapshot" (Y:4833), and the task line asks for "`n` and generation". The client writes them to `.head`'s `n=` and `generation=` (CLI44:694–695). Form 4 takes its generation from `.head` (CLI44:745), and the offer's `act` is built from that `n` and generation (L5:364). SVC-7's own words give the answer's head as `text <n>` alone (Y:4064–4066). Lane 3's ruling makes that an erratum, ERR-26: Y:4064–4066's head reads "`text <n>` with the register generation that transaction read" (Y:4833) (#165 6746215510, OP-2). The bytes are OP-3's: section 13.4's head line, `text <n> <generation>`, which lane 1's U5 review passes (#165 6747151224).
- **Fresh at `n`.** It answers `text <n>` only for that transaction's `n`, so the files of one answer are never from two seqs (Y:4064–4065). It "never answers from images it read before" (Y:4088; svc:23).
- **Where the images come from** (Y:4043–4050):
  - images it holds in memory with the digests at `n` are answered;
  - otherwise it brings them to `n` with the journal entries read in that transaction, by the function the writers' snapshot uses, and checks them against the digests;
  - on a mismatch it takes the file from the per-environment image cache, where the cache holds it at the digest at `n`;
  - otherwise it answers `unavailable`, and fetches the file whole in the background, in a transaction of its own, never on a request's timer: on the one held connection, between requests, with its own bounds (section 7; OP-15, closed).
- **The write-through.** What it fetched goes to the per-environment image cache, the one cache the writers' snapshot reads (Y:4054–4062; tasks:1418). It writes through T302's cache API, in the U5 gate's words (L1:457; lane 1's U5 re-check, N-e, #165 6752336451).
- **Its contents:** `text <n>`, then for each file its path, its byte count, its digest and its bytes (Y:4065–4066); a set's members are files like any other, and the list is the render set's paths, each with its digest at `n` (CLI47:665; section 13.4). The digest is the database's at `n` (Y:4070–4072): the `sha256` of `journal_files` (Y:2394), which `.head`'s `file=` rows also carry (CLI47:699). The bytes are section 13.4's proposal.
- **The client's checks.** It checks each file against the digest in the same answer, which catches a cut or corrupted transfer (Y:4072–4074). Then it writes the cache family at `n`, each file in the form its read site expects, `.head` last (Y:4142–4151; CLI44:675–688), and exits 0 (Y:4341).

### 5.2 `refuse db <cause>`

- **Only where the failure is definitive in this request:** "the connection refused, an authentication error, the switch not `primary`, the schema version refused", with a fixed cause and the SQLSTATE (Y:4082–4085). It carries fixed phrases and the SQLSTATE only, never `psql`'s message text (Y:3797–3799).
- **The four causes.** Y names two cause words, `auth` and `unreachable` (Y:3931–3932). Lane 1's answer names all four: `<cause>` is one of `unreachable`, `auth`, `switch-files` and `schema` (#165 6746620707, OP-4; CLI47:767). Lane 5 reads them as Y:4083–4085's four definitive failures, in their order: the connection refused, an authentication error, the switch not `primary`, the schema version refused. Only these four are sent; any other word is malformed, so `unavailable` (lane 1's U5 review, M2; section 13.5).
- **Definitive in this request.** Lane 1 reads Y:4082–4091 as letting the service answer `unavailable` whenever the request itself did not see the cause, such as a past background connect's outcome, so that needs no ruling (6746620707, OP-4). The direct read then decides, and the outcome is the same 69 or 2 (Y:4089–4091).
  - **`switch-files` and `schema` are always the request's own.** The request's transaction reads the switch and checks the window (section 5.1), so a switch other than `primary` answers `refuse db switch-files -`, and a number outside the window answers `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>`, each definitive in the request's own transaction (Y:4082–4085; lane 1's U5 review, M2).
  - **`auth` and `unreachable` are sent only where this request met them.** Otherwise the answer is `unavailable` (Y:4086–4091; lane 1's U5 review, M2).
  - **No request meets them, so this service never sends them** (the Codex review, BLOCKING 5). A refused connection and an authentication error are met only at a connect, and the child connects in the background, never on a request's time (section 7; Y:4014–4016, Y:4024–4025). So each is a past background attempt's outcome, which lane 1 reads as `unavailable` (6746620707, OP-4), and the direct read then decides, with the same 69 or 2 (Y:4089–4091). T512 builds it so: its request path sends neither word (`lanes-index`:8547–8593 at line R's merge106). The two words stay in the format (13.5), since lane 1's contract names four causes (CLI47:767), and a client that reads one refuses as this section says.
- **Every failure a request meets, and its answer** (the Codex review, BLOCKING 5; as T512 builds it, `lanes-index`:8377–8409, :8532–8593 and :8746–8797 at line R's merge106, and the timer's path at T512's `88b50886`, :8411–8458 and :8595–8625). `refuse db` comes only from a value the request's own transaction read, never from a connect, a lost child or an SQLSTATE:
  - the switch read as other than `primary`: `refuse db switch-files -`;
  - `REGISTER_SCHEMA_VERSION` outside `readers_from` to `version`: `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>`;
  - the per-request timer, whether the request's query is running or the request is still queued: `unavailable slow`, sent at the expiry; a running query's child is ended only where its marker has not come within 100 ms of the `SIGINT`, and is otherwise kept, its transaction rolled back (section 6.2). T512's round 5 answers a queued request at its own expiry, as built at `1e1846e3`, where T512 at `bb19b1ad` did not while the worker ran an earlier job (section 6.2);
  - no held child, or one still connecting, at the start, after a restart or after a configuration change: `unavailable` (sections 7 and 8.4);
  - a request queued or running under an earlier configuration epoch (section 8.4): `unavailable` (lane 1's U5 NIT-f, #165 6881937840; `lanes-index`:9078 and :8765 at T512's `26c77534`); and a request still running when another request has seen a change the worker has not yet taken: `unavailable` (section 8.4; as built at T512's `f21ca4a7`, `lanes-index`:8777, but for the gap section 8.4 states, which T512 at `bb19b1ad` still left, and which its round 5's gate closes, as built at `1e1846e3`);
  - a job of the worker that fails in any other way: `unavailable`, and the held child is replaced at once (lane 1's gate, S2, #165 6881993079; `lanes-index`:8656–8667 at T512's `26c77534`);
  - the child's end of stream or exit, or a write to it that fails, before the request's marker, a connection lost during the query among them where `psql` ends on it: `unavailable`, and the child is ended and started again in the background (section 7);
  - a statement that fails, whatever its SQLSTATE: the server's `lock_timeout` or `statement_timeout`, a connection lost during the query where `psql` reports it as a failed statement, a privilege refused, or any other: `unavailable`. The service rolls the transaction back, and writes the fixed phrase `a request failed: SQLSTATE <sqlstate>`, with `-` for a value that is not five of `[0-9A-Z]`, to `register.log` (Y:3797–3799). A rollback that fails ends the child, as above;
  - rows it cannot read: no opening marker, bytes that are not UTF-8, a count of results that does not match, or a head below 0: `unavailable`;
  - a cache miss, a path the lines cannot carry, or an answer over 16,777,216 bytes: `unavailable` (13.4);
  - its install changed, or a configuration change that ends it: `unavailable restarting`, the second within section 8.4's two limits, where the request is answered plain `unavailable`, and the service exits (section 8.4, "A change that ends it"; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, SHOULD 1, #165 6885771975).
- **The client refuses exactly as the direct read would** (Y:4085–4086), and makes no direct read after it. `register read` exits 69, in form 2 too (Y:4343; CLI44:775). The guard then makes SVC-12's declared-outage check, and otherwise refuses with 2 (Y:4343; svc:23).
- **What the client prints.** Under `--for guard`, the first stderr line on that 69 is `lanes-index: refuse db <cause> <SQLSTATE>`, with `-` where there is no SQLSTATE, built from the answer's fixed cause and SQLSTATE, never `psql`'s message text (CLI47:767; Y:3797–3799). A direct read that found the switch not `primary`, or the number outside the window, prints the same line, with `-` for the SQLSTATE, before any other; every other failed direct read keeps lane 1's §2.6 line 1 first (CLI47:767). No fallback line follows a `refuse db` (section 11.2).
- **The second line, for `schema`,** is as CLI47:767 has it: the next stderr line names the client's `REGISTER_SCHEMA_VERSION`, the database's `readers_from` and `version`, and the remedy Y:2338 names for the case, so that the guard's and the operator's text is the direct read's. The remedy is "upgrade the tools" where the client's number is below `readers_from`, and "the database is behind: run the bootstrap" where it is above `version` (Y:2338; lane 1's U5 review, 1(b)). Through the service, the answer carries the two database numbers (section 13.5), and T113 prints the same line on the direct path.
- **A rollback's declaration** counts only where the cause is `switch-files`, by either path (Y:4449–4454; CLI47:767), so SVC-12's condition 2 reads one fact.
- **The line's bytes** are lane 1's (lane 1's U5 review, M2 and 1(b), #165 6747151224), confirmed by lane 3 (#165 6747313608): `refuse db <cause> <sqlstate>`, four fields, for `unreachable`, `auth` and `switch-files`, and `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>`, six fields, for `schema` (section 13.5).

### 5.3 `unavailable`

- **When the service answers it:**
  - on any timeout (Y:4086–4087);
  - at its per-request timer, as `unavailable slow` (Y:4012–4013), whether the request's query is running or the request is still queued behind another's (section 6.2; lane 1's U5 review, S1);
  - before its `psql` child has connected, at the start, after a restart, or after a configuration change (Y:4014–4016; lane 3's ruling, #165 6827356485, point 3; sections 7 and 8.4);
  - on a lost child, a failed statement of any SQLSTATE, or rows it cannot read (section 5.2);
  - on a cache miss the image cache cannot fill (Y:4046–4049);
  - at once, to a request that arrives during the background whole-file fetch (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-15; section 7);
  - when its install has changed, as `unavailable restarting` (Y:3916–3918), and when a configuration change ends it, the same way, within section 8.4's two limits, where it answers plain `unavailable` (section 8.4, "A change that ends it"; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, SHOULD 1, #165 6885771975).
- **What the client counts as `unavailable` too:**
  - no socket (`ENOENT`), or a dead one (`ECONNREFUSED`) (Y:3881–3882);
  - a failed directory or length check (Y:3764–3765, Y:3778–3779);
  - an owner file of another pid namespace or `boot_id`, on a socket that answers the connect; the client then starts none (Y:3784–3788; section 2.4);
  - an owner file the client cannot use, on a socket that answers the connect: absent, unreadable, longer than 4,096 bytes or not UTF-8; with a last byte that is not `\n`, an empty file among them; with a known key on two lines; with no `install=` line, or one whose value is not "three or six runs of ASCII digits, one space between each, and nothing else", rule 4's exact form in section 2.4 (follow-up 1's part 2, which lane 3's ruling takes, #165 6894116246), as a service that cannot `stat` its own file, or its module, writes it; or with no `pidns=` or `boot=` line where the client has its own; the client then starts none (lane 3's rulings, #165 6879724890, point 1, and #165 6880543448, OP-19 (b); section 2.4);
  - a server of another uid (Y:3792–3794);
  - a service that accepts a connection and never answers (svc:117);
  - "a bad header, a byte count that does not match, a digest that does not match, a file it did not ask for, an answer over 16 MB, or one not complete within the 300 ms share" (Y:4076–4078). Section 13.7 lists each case under section 13's format;
  - an answer whose items disagree on a path, "a mismatch" in Y's words (Y:4076; 13.4).
- **What the client does.** It discards all of the answer and reads directly (Y:4079), within what its budget has left (Y:4087–4088), and writes `.direct` (Y:4153–4154). The guard's client prints the fallback line once, whether that direct read succeeds or fails (section 11.2).

### 5.4 From answer to exit

The client's exits through the socket, from Y's exit mapping (Y:4336–4343) and lane 1's forms (CLI44:769–779):

| What happened | A reader's `register read` | `--for guard`, form 2 | The guard |
|---|---|---|---|
| the text at `n`, through the service | 0 | 0, files cached | parses; decides 0 or 2 |
| `unavailable`, then a direct read that succeeded | 0 | 0, files cached, and `guard: direct (service unavailable)` on stderr | parses; decides 0 or 2 |
| `refuse db <cause>` | 69 | 69; first on stderr, `lanes-index: refuse db <cause> <SQLSTATE>`, and for `schema` the numbers' line next; no fallback line | SVC-12's declared-outage check; otherwise 2 |
| `unavailable`, then a direct read that failed | 69 | 69; first on stderr, the direct read's cause line, and for `schema` the numbers' line next, then `guard: direct (service unavailable)` | SVC-12's declared-outage check; otherwise 2 |

The stderr in the last two rows is lane 1's answer (#165 6746620707, OP-4 and OP-11; CLI46:764, CLI47:767), and section 11.2 gives it. The numbers' line is CLI47:767's second line (section 5.2). The last row is draft 2's `unavailable`-then-failed row, with the fallback line that CLI46:764 prints on every fallback (lane 1's U5 review, M3). The other rows (too late, mode `index`, Q6(c)'s 78, and any other failure) are not the socket's, and stay as Y:4344–4347 and CLI44:769–782 give them.

## 6. The limits

### 6.1 The client's wait: 300 ms

- **300 ms, connect and answer together,** or the service's timer plus 100 ms where SVC-6 raises that timer (Y:4182; tasks:1412). So it is 300 ms with no `register_rtt_ms=`, or one of 100 or less, and `register_rtt_ms=` plus 200 ms above that (CLI44:301): 350 ms at 150 (tp:1966–1971), and 656 ms at Eagle's p90 of 456 ms (Y:4193–4195).
- **At its end** the client stops, discards what it read, and reads directly: an answer "not complete within the 300 ms share" is `unavailable` (Y:4076–4079). A silent service costs one such wait a prompt, "never one per read", because the first fallback writes `.direct` (Y:4153–4156).
- **It is one share of the guard's 5 s,** spent once (svc:124; Y:4182). The direct read after it has `T0 + 3.75 s − now` (Y:4184), and a read through the service that completes after `T0 + 3.75 s` is too late, as any read is (Y:4185). So under `--for guard` the wait ends at the earlier of its start plus `w_s` and `T0 + 3.75 s`, where its start is just before the client's checks and its `connect()` (section 13.8; lane 1's U5 review, N4; `lanes-index`:9867–9869 at T512's `bb19b1ad`).
- **A reader waits the same** `w_s`, at most once a `register read`, and never again in an invocation once `.direct` is written (Y:5443; Y:4153–4156; lane 1's U5 review, N3). Form 3 asks the socket too (section 11.1), so a process whose form 1 was answered can wait again at form 3; `bound_s` has room for it, at least one read above `B` (Y:5460).
- **The guard never waits for a start** (Y:3921–3925; section 8.2).

### 6.2 The service's timer: 200 ms

- **200 ms,** or `register_rtt_ms=` plus 100 ms where `register_rtt_ms=` is over 100 ms; with none, or 0, it is 200 ms (Y:4001–4002, Y:4008; D9's row, Y:3519).
- **`register_rtt_ms=` is a p90:** "the p90 of act 3's `select 1` round trips in one session, taken while the workstation is at work and written when it joins, as `c` and `N_a` are" (Y:4005–4008; text 8″a). Act 3 takes "the median, and the p90, which is `register_rtt_ms=`" (Y:5915; text 8″b). Text 8″ changed only how act 3 measures the input, not the timer's rule (tasks:1408).
- **100 ms shorter than the client's wait,** so that the service's `unavailable` normally reaches the client before the client gives up (Y:4183). The two do not start together, so this is not an order the code guarantees: the client's wait starts before its checks of the directory and the path's length and before its `connect()` (`lanes-index`:9867, :9521–9531 at T512's `bb19b1ad`), while the service's timer starts only once the whole request has arrived (:8985–8990; and :9061–9072, for a request that waits on a change). Where more than 100 ms passes before the request arrives, or the guard's cut comes sooner (section 6.1; :9868–9869), the client's wait can end first. The client then gives up and reads directly, as at the end of any wait (section 6.1), which is safe, since the direct read is the fallback. The contract states this order, and does not carry the client's deadline to the service, by lane 5's choice (lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, SHOULD 2, #165 6885771975).
- **The server's bounds** per request, set in `PGOPTIONS`: `lock_timeout` 150 ms and `statement_timeout` 180 ms, so that the server cancels its own query just inside the 200 ms timer (Y:4017–4020, Y:3976).
- **On expiry** the service sends `SIGINT` to `psql`, which cancels the query, and answers `unavailable slow`. If `psql` has not printed the request's marker within 100 ms more, the service kills it; the next request starts a new one in the background, and is answered `unavailable` until it connects. "A reconnect never runs on a request's time" (Y:4012–4016).
  - **As built** (the Codex re-review, S6; T512's `88b50886`, `lanes-index`:8411–8458 and :8619): the service answers `unavailable slow` at the expiry itself, with the `SIGINT`, not after the 100 ms. It ends the child only where the marker has not come within those 100 ms. A child whose marker comes is kept, and its transaction is rolled back only where the marker's SQLSTATE is not `00000`, by the rule for a failed statement (section 5.2): a statement that fails, or is cancelled, leaves its transaction open, while `00000` is the SQLSTATE of the whole query having run, from its `BEGIN` to its `COMMIT` (section 5.1), so nothing is left open to roll back (lane 1's U5 NIT-j, #165 6881937840; `run` and `rollback`, `lanes-index`:8435–8438 at `88b50886`, :8553–8599 at T512's `26c77534`). A rollback that fails, or whose own marker's SQLSTATE is not `00000`, ends the child.
  - **What `psql` does with the `SIGINT`.** T512's writer found that `psql` 16.15, reading its commands on a pipe, ends itself after the cancel, with exit code 3, before it prints the marker (T512's round at `88b50886`, as lane 5's coordinator reports it). So with that `psql`, every expiry ends the child, and the next request starts a new one, as Y's rule gives for a child whose marker has not come. The kept child is the rule for a `psql` that outlives its cancel.
- **`SIGINT` goes only to the request whose query `psql` is running,** with Y:4013–4016's 100 ms kill rule. The service takes connections concurrently but sends one query at a time (Y:3982–3984), and each request's timer runs from its arrival (section 13.3), so a request can expire while it is still queued behind another's query. Such a request is answered `unavailable slow` and dropped from the queue, with no `SIGINT`, which would cancel the other request's query or the background fetch (lane 1's U5 review, S1, #165 6747151224). As built at T512's `1e1846e3`, its round 5 (commit `ba1d4a4d`): at the request's arrival plus its timer, a job still queued is removed from the queue under `cond` and answered `unavailable slow`, with no `SIGINT`, and the worker skips a job already answered; a job the worker has begun is the worker's to answer, at its timer, with the `SIGINT`, and the connection waits at most the kill's 100 ms more for that answer before it gives `unavailable slow` itself (`request`, `lanes-index`:9206–9225; `serve_job`, :8774–8775). At `bb19b1ad` a queued request was answered `unavailable slow` only when the worker reached it, or at its arrival plus its timer and 1.1 s more, past the client's wait, so the client first gave up and read directly (Codex's review of T512 at `bb19b1ad`, SHOULD 2; `lanes-index`:8773–8776 and :9145–9167 there). A request that arrives during the background fetch is answered `unavailable` at once (OP-15; section 7).
- **From the request's arrival.** AT-10 reads `unavailable slow` "no earlier than 200 ms after the request reached it", and no earlier than 250 ms at `register_rtt_ms=150` (tp:1966–1970).
- **Not yet shown:** "the 150, 180, 200 and 100 ms values are the design's, for lane 2's fixture to prove" (Y:4026–4029).

### 6.3 16 MB

- **The client reads at most 16 MB,** and stops at its wait, whichever comes first (Y:4079–4080; tasks:1414).
- **An answer over 16 MB is `unavailable`:** the client discards all of it and reads directly (Y:4076–4079).
- **The service sends none over it, and builds none past it.** It counts a text answer as it builds it, and answers `unavailable` at the first record or line that would carry it past 16,777,216 bytes, building none after it (13.4; lane 3's ruling, #165 6879724890, point 3; the Codex review, SHOULD 4).
- **For scale:** the real `lanes/LANES.md` is 2,590,927 bytes (Y:4050), and a 1.35 MB answer took 2.35 ms median from the connect to the last byte (Y:4066–4068).
- **What the 16 MB counts, and its unit,** are section 13.6's proposal: every byte the client reads from the socket for one answer, against 16·2^20 bytes (OP-3).

## 7. The service's connection

- **One long-lived `psql` child on pipes,** logged in as the reader login, run as `psql -X -q -A -t -v ON_ERROR_STOP=0 -v VERBOSITY=sqlstate` (Y:3971–3973). No database driver, and `dependencies = []` stays (Y:3979–3981; svc:34). This is the service's own argv, not the one-shot argv of `lanes-index`'s register commands (CLI44:205–206), which lane 1's §2.2 gives every register call with `VERBOSITY=verbose` (CLI410:206); only the service's child runs `VERBOSITY=sqlstate` (lane 1's post, #165 6752661856, point 2).
- **Its credential:** `reader_url=` and `reader_passfile=` (Y:3460; CLI44:277), read from the 0600 configuration and its password file (Y:3889–3891).
  - **`PGPASSFILE` is the READER's own file.** The child carries `PGPASSFILE` equal to the configured `reader_passfile=`, the READER login's 0600 password file, never another role's, or with none configured, today's no-password file; and its environment carries no literal password, no connection URL and no `PGPASSWORD`. That is lane 3's ruling 4 (#165 6878977318), under which design.md's service section and `contracts/config.md:108` stand (F18-config:108), and each direct-read `psql` child carries its own the same way. T512 sets it so: the READER's file is taken for `register_reader` alone, and every inherited `PG` variable is dropped before `PGPASSFILE` is set (`lanes-index`:4695–4708, :4752–4768 and :8253–8263 at line R's merge106).
- **Its connection is in its environment, never its argv.** The URL's host, port, database and user, and its `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `target_session_attrs` and `connect_timeout`, go to `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGDATABASE`, `PGUSER`, `PGSSLMODE`, `PGSSLROOTCERT`, `PGSSLCERT`, `PGSSLKEY`, `PGTARGETSESSIONATTRS` and `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT`; `PGPASSFILE` and `PGOPTIONS` are the service's own (Y:3799–3812). A password in the URL, or any other query parameter, makes `serve` exit with the stamp `config-refused`, and clients read directly (Y:3812–3815).
- **The mapping is T103's `pg_url_env`, after every inherited `PG` variable is dropped.** `serve` maps the URL with T103's `pg_url_env(url, what)`, SVC-2's one mapping, which `serve` reuses (lane 1's T103, `a3e1ddee`, #165 6747233899). Before it, every inherited variable whose name begins `PG` is dropped, so `psql` gets only what the configuration gives it: the URL's mapping (SVC-2), the configured `PGPASSFILE`, and `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` at `c` (Y:4970); beside them stand the server bounds the service sets itself in `PGOPTIONS` (Y:3976, Y:4017–4020), never an inherited `PGOPTIONS`. That is lane 3's ruling on register paths, which names lane 5's `serve`, through `pg_url_env`, among them (#165 6747611042); B6 records it as ERR-39, an erratum to Y:4970, and contract draft 4.10 states it (CLI410:214). The service's `psql` also gets `PGAPPNAME`, set to `lanes-index`, as T103's `_env` sets it on every path (`lanes-index:1756` at T105 `6928ffb`; CLI410:216; `config.md:110`; lane 1's U5 re-check, #165 6752336451), and its only outcome is `application_name` in `pg_stat_activity` (lane 1's U5 re-check, F1). Round B8's ERR-39 now lists, beside the URL's mapping, the configured `PGPASSFILE`, `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` at `c` and `PGOPTIONS` with the call's bounds (Y:3503), and round B9 adds `PGAPPNAME`, set to `lanes-index` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752360829).
- **The connect: `c` for each address.** `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` is `c` for each of the endpoint's `N_a` addresses (Y:3974–3975; text 13, at Y:3923–3925; D9's row, Y:3519), so one connect may take `N_a`·`c` (Y:3507).
  - `c` is the URL's `connect_timeout=`, the connect bound each workstation writes from its own p90 when it joins; with none it is 2, and a value under 2 is refused (Y:3810–3812, Y:5877).
  - `N_a` is `register_addresses=`, default 1 (Y:5877).
  - On register paths `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` is set to `c`, not defaulted, so an inherited value cannot lengthen it (Y:4970).
  - Y:3892 (W:3857) still says the service "overrides with 2". Lane 3's ruling makes that an erratum, ERR-25: Y:3892's "which the service overrides with 2" reads "which the service, as every register path, sets to `c` (SVC-6)", as every other place says (#165 6746215510, OP-1).
- **In the background only.** The child starts by the service's first request at the latest; T512 may start it as soon as it listens, which is SVC-4's warm start. It restarts lazily after a failure met while serving, at the next request (Y:3971–3973; Y:4014–4016), and at once after a failed idle check (below; lane 3's ruling, #165 6834196887). That is lane 3's ruling, which reads Y:3971–3973's "at the service's first request" as "by then, at the latest", with lane 2's guard cases for a cache miss and for the idle check's `[interval]` as its reason, since each needs the connection held before the first request (#165 6827356485, point 3, on lane 5's T512 plan, #165 6827295319, point 3). Its connect runs "outside any share, so that no request waits for it" (svc:124; Y:4024–4025).
  - **The connect at listen starts no fetch.** The background whole-file fetch below starts only at a cache miss the image cache cannot fill (section 5.1; Y:4046–4050).
  - **The `SessionStart` block is unchanged.** The connect is the service's own, in the background, so the block still only starts the service and asks it nothing (section 1; section 9; lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, #165 6828035829).
  - **A `psql` lost after the start** is SVC-6's failure: there is no stamp, since only the causes that make `serve` exit before it listens are stamped (Y:3926–3927; section 8.6); the next request starts a new child in the background, as after any failure met while serving (Y:3971–3973; Y:4014–4016; lane 3's ruling, #165 6833590985); where a failed idle check finds it first, a new child starts at once (below; lane 3's ruling, #165 6834196887); and a request is answered `unavailable` until it connects (Y:4014–4016; section 5.3). 007 has no test for it (lane 3's ruling, #165 6754633102; ERR-45), and ERR-45 says that T237 may follow in a later round (lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, N-7, #165 6828035829).
  - **A connect that fails, the first one included** (the Codex re-review, S3; as built, `lanes-index`:8359–8388, :8389–8409 and :8459–8503 at T512's `88b50886`, the same logic as merge106's). A connect that fails, whether the connect at listen, a restart's or any other, leaves the child down until a request comes: the next request starts the next connect in the background, and is answered `unavailable` (section 5.3). A failed idle check and a configuration change start one at once instead (below; section 8.4). The service starts no connect sooner than 1 s after the last one it started, so a database that stays down costs at most one `psql` a second; only a configuration change clears that wait (`lanes-index`:7869–7877 and :8473–8474; at T512's `26c77534`, :7915–7920, and :9058, where the worker re-reads the change). A connect fails on any error, not only an operating system's: at T512's `26c77534` an exception of any kind in the connect, a password file that cannot be made among them, is a failed connect, so the child never stays "connecting" (lane 1's gate, S2, #165 6881993079; `connect`, `lanes-index`:8493–8529).
- **Where it connects:** the primary's port 5432, directly, through the install's load balancer, never through a pooler (Y:3976–3979; text 15; R51).
- **One query at a time,** one round trip a request. The service handles connections concurrently, but each query, ended by a per-request marker that `psql` prints, goes to the one `psql` in turn (Y:3982–3988). The marker's form is the next sub-bullet's.
  - **The marker, as built** (the Codex re-review, S5; `lanes-index`:7899–7925 and :8324–8358 at T512's `88b50886`, as at merge106). Each exchange ends with `\warn` of the per-request marker and `:SQLSTATE`, which `psql` prints on its standard error and never sends to the server, and the service reads `psql`'s output until that line; the request's own rows open with a marker of their own on standard output. Y:3983 says `\echo`, which prints on standard output. Y's point stands, a per-request marker that `psql` prints and the service reads to, and `\warn` adds the exchange's SQLSTATE, by which a failed statement is told from a whole answer (section 5.2). Lane 3's ruling keeps `\warn … :SQLSTATE`, on standard error, as built: standard output carries the answer, so no answer line can read as a marker (#165 6880934796; the Codex delta review, S5). Lane 3 records the erratum on Y:3983's `\echo` as ERR-57, at the follow-up's commit 24 `f9d6069e`, `specs/007-sql-register/research.md`:1803, with `openspec/changes/add-sql-register/design.md`:4030–4031 and :4590 aligned (#165 6881380726).
- **Connections at once: at most 16** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879724890, point 4; the Codex review, SHOULD 5; as built, `lanes-index`:7879–7897, :8226–8239, :8759–8792 and :8914 at T512's `88b50886`, and :7922–7941, :8325–8354 and :8936–8971 at T512's `26c77534`). The service listens with a backlog of 64, and holds at most 16 accepted connections at once, each on a thread of its own. It closes a connection accepted past the 16th at once, with no answer and no byte read, and writes the fixed phrase `a connection past the cap was closed` to `register.log`; that client meets the end of the stream with no byte, or a send that fails, either of which is `unavailable` (13.7), so it reads directly and writes `.direct`, and the close itself starts nothing. Each connection held is bounded in time: its request within `w_s` of the accept (13.3), its answer within the request's timer, the kill's 100 ms and 1 s more, and its send within `w_s` of the send's start (13.4). A connection's slot is given back however its thread ends and however its close ends, and a connection whose thread cannot start is closed with no answer and gives its slot back, with the fixed phrase `a connection's thread could not start` (Codex SHOULD 2 of lane 3's review of `88b50886`; `lanes-index`:8325–8354 and :8966–8971 at T512's `26c77534`, its commit `584bea6a`). N is 16, lane 5's choice, for three reasons, as T512's round gives them (`lanes-index`:7879–7896; :7922–7941 at `26c77534`):
  - **memory:** each connection holds at most one answer, which the service counts as it builds and never lets past 16 MiB (13.4), and a request of at most 64 KiB (13.3), so 16 hold at most 16 times (16 MiB + 64 KiB), 257 MiB of payload, at the worst, before Python's own overhead (`lanes-index`:7930 at T512's `26c77534`); today's largest answer, `--all`, is about 8 MB, `LANES.md`'s 2,590,927 bytes and 39 logs' 5,182,523, so 16 hold about 128 MB;
  - **clients:** one uid's environment runs its five lanes, each asking at most a guard's prefetch and a reader's read or two at once, about 15; and the one `psql` child takes one query at a time within each request's 200 ms timer (section 6.2), so a burst far past 16 would be answered `unavailable slow` anyway, and closing it at once spares each such client its wait;
  - **the backlog:** 64 is four times 16, so the kernel queues a burst, which the service then takes or closes at once.
- **A client whose connect is not taken** (`lanes-index`:9156–9159 at T512's `88b50886`): a connect that fails other than by `ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED`, a full backlog among them, or that is not accepted within the client's wait, is unavailable, and the client reads directly and starts none (13.8).
- **The background whole-file fetch** (section 5.1), on a cache miss the image cache cannot fill (Y:4046–4050):
  - **One connection.** The fetch runs on the held connection, in its own transaction, between requests (Y:3714; Y:4048–4050), so lane 1's install formula, one connection per environment, stands (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-15).
  - **A request that arrives during the fetch** is answered `unavailable` at once, and a request's `SIGINT` (Y:4012–4016) never cancels the fetch (6746620707, OP-15).
  - **Its bounds:** `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '1000ms'` and `statement_timeout = '10s'`, the renderer's row (Y:3527), with an 11 s timer. On expiry the fetch is dropped, and the next miss retries. That is lane 3's ruling, as lane 1 suggested, and B4 records it as ERR-29, an erratum that adds a D9 row for the fetch (#165 6746655077, OP-15).
- **The idle check.** While no request comes, the service sends `SELECT 1` on the held connection every 60 s, under the same timer, and a failed idle check starts a new child in the background at once, so that the next prompt's request finds it connected (Y:3995–4000; lane 3's ruling, #165 6834196887). Y's "lazily" governs a child lost while serving, which the next request finds (Y:4014–4016; above). The 60 s `SELECT 1` runs from the child's connect, whether or not a request has come, so a service that connected as it listened, and that no reader has asked yet, is checked too (lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, #165 6828035829; lane 3's ruling, #165 6827356485, point 3). So the connection is never idle for the 4 minutes after which the load balancer resets a flow (Y:3995–4000; text 16). Section 10 gives the test-only interval.
- **The server's TCP keepalives** keep it open too: every 60 s in lane 1's install contract (Y:3997–3998), set by the install as `tcp_keepalives_idle: "60"`, `tcp_keepalives_interval: "10"` and `tcp_keepalives_count: "6"`, so that no connection is silent for the load balancer's 4-minute idle reset, the service's held connection among them (Y-ops:143). They are the server's parameters, which the install sets, not the service (Y-ops:143; lane 1's U5 review, N8).
- **What it never does:** write, render or re-index; read the mirror or the outage declaration (Y:4030–4032); read a pid, a `/proc` entry of a lane, a session record or tmux (Y:4436–4437).

## 8. The start on demand

No supervisor of its own: no systemd and no launchd (svc:41; Y:3872–3876).

### 8.1 Who starts it

- **The `SessionStart` block,** on a primary workstation, after its own answer, without waiting, through `serve --start` (Y:3878–3881; section 9).
- **Any client** that finds no socket (`ENOENT`), a dead one (`ECONNREFUSED`), or an owner file from another install, unless a failure stamp is fresh (Y:3881–3883). Item 1 also names an owner file from another namespace, which SVC-2 contradicts. Lane 3's ruling: SVC-2 governs, so a client that finds an owner file from another pid namespace or another boot on a socket that answers reads directly and starts none, and Y:3882–3883's start list drops "another namespace or", as an erratum in B4, ERR-27 (#165 6746655077, OP-8, correcting 6746215510; section 2.4). Nor does a client start one that finds, on a socket that answers, an owner file it cannot use (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879724890, point 1; section 2.4): an owner file names another install only where the client can use it. And a client starts one for another install only where the server's uid is its own: the uid check wins (lane 5's ruling on lane 1's U5 NIT-h, #165 6881986067; section 2.4).
- **Never form 4,** which never asks the socket (B7), **and never a writer** (Y:3829–3831).

### 8.2 How

- **From Python, never bash.** The starter runs `lanes-index serve` detached: `subprocess.Popen(..., start_new_session=True)`, which is `setsid`; standard input and output on `/dev/null`, standard error to `register.log`; working directory `/` (Y:3884–3887; tasks:1396). "No bash code detaches anything" (Y:3892–3893). **By the launcher's path as invoked** (follow-up 1's part 5, which corrects part 4; lane 3's ruling, "`:7761`: close it", #165 6894116246): the `serve` it starts is the launcher's `__file__` as Python gives it, made absolute where it is not, by `os.path.join(os.getcwd(), __file__)` (a fallback: on Python 3.9 and later `__main__`'s `__file__` is absolute in practice, `./lanes-index` giving `<dir>/./lanes-index`, so the join is not taken for the launcher run as a script, lane 5's reviewer, cases B and B2; it stays relative where Python's start-up cannot fit the working directory in its `PATH_MAX` buffer, as in a working directory longer than `PATH_MAX`; there the join applies, and `os.getcwd()` does get it, by glibc's walk of every ancestor, which takes seconds (opensoft/openRepoTools#165 comment 6895648101), lane 1 finding it relative in a working directory 4,582 bytes long, #165 6895008151, NIT 2; the joined path is then itself longer than `PATH_MAX`, which the kernel refuses, so the `serve` started cannot open it, lane 5's probe at 4,744 bytes giving "File name too long"), and never normalized, as T512's commits 4 and 5 build it: commit 5, `4e343ea2`, gives that path in `register_serve_launch_file` (`lanes-index`:8132–8143 at `4e343ea2`), which `register_serve_launch` (:7747) starts (:7784), and so corrects commit 4, `e2212236`, which started `os.path.realpath(__file__)`; at `23cb3799` the launch was `os.path.abspath(__file__)` (:7761 there). The reason: `abspath` joins `a/link/../lanes-index` lexically, and so names another launcher's file, or none, while the kernel resolves the un-normalized path physically, `link/..` included, to the file the client runs (lane 1's R2 review, NIT 1, #165 6892234131; lane 5's test on Python 3.12.3, where `__main__`'s `__file__` is absolute but not normalized). The install identity still takes that path's real path, `os.path.realpath(__file__)` (section 2.4; `register_serve_install`, `lanes-index`:8176 at T512's `4e343ea2`, :8138 at `23cb3799`), and the service's own `__file__` keeps any link in the path the launcher was invoked by, made absolute against the physical working directory, so its install check on each request still notices a link to the launcher repointed to another launcher, as before, where the service was started through that link; a link in the working directory's own path is not kept, since Python joins a relative invocation to the physical working directory, so `cd dirlink; ./lanes-index` gives `<real>/./lanes-index`, and a repoint of `dirlink` is not seen (lane 1's U5 NIT 1, #165 6895008151); a service started by its real path, as `lanes-edit.sh` starts every one, does not, a v1.3 limit (section 8.4; OP-20, closed). Under T124's form, the module's code runs in the launcher's globals, so `__file__` there is the launcher's path as invoked, made absolute against the physical working directory where it was relative, and the service started is the launcher, not the module: lane 1's R2 review finds that code running in the launcher's namespace, its `__globals__` being `vars(__main__)` (#165 6892234131, question 1); and, as lane 5's account, the T512 writer's probe on a copy of T124's form finds `co_filename` alone naming `lanes_index_main.py`, and the started `serve`'s path, through `a/link/../lanes-index`, that same string, with its Run C, 110 passed and 2 skipped. Guard-fetch's detached child, `lanes-index register guard-fetch`, which is not the service, is started the same way, by `register_serve_launch_file()`, since `os.path.abspath(__file__)` joined its path lexically too (T507's A5, `705fd7ec`): T512's commit 7, `9d44830f`, builds it (`register_guard_fetch`, `lanes-index`:6833, its argument list at :6861, at T512's `4444ddc4`, where `register_serve_launch` is :7756, its start :7820, and `register_serve_launch_file` :8168).
- **A reduced environment:** `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, the `XDG_*` variables and `LANES_INDEX_CONFIG` (Y:3887–3888), and section 10's two test-only variables where they are set (R37; tasks:1427; research.md DEC-15). No password and no URL is in it (Y:3888–3889; svc:177).
- **Outside the hook's process group.** The new session survives a group kill of the hook, and a timed-out hook's SIGTERM (Y:3893–3898).
- **The guard never waits for a start.** A guard that finds no service starts one, and takes the direct read for that prompt; readers do the same (Y:3921–3925). Waiting for the new service's connect could take its `connect_timeout`, `c` for each of the `N_a` addresses, "which the budget cannot give" (Y:3923–3925; text 13).
  - **So the call that starts it is answered by the direct read.** svc:43–45 and User Story 8's fourth scenario (F18-spec:734–735) say that the service starts on the first call "and answers it". This contract follows Y:3921–3925: the call that starts a service reads directly, and the service answers from the next call. The Codex review finds the same side right, since waiting through `N_a`·`c` cannot fit the prompt's budget (SHOULD 3). Lane 3's ruling takes it as an erratum on both texts (#165 6879724890, point 2), and the follow-up's commit 20 makes it (#165 6879909534).
  - **A start no client makes.** A client that finds an owner file it cannot use, or one of another namespace or boot, on a socket that answers, runs no starter at all (sections 2.4 and 8.1).

### 8.3 One instance: `register.lock`

- **One instance per environment.** `lanes-index serve` takes `flock` on `register.lock` without waiting, and holds it for its whole life (Y:3899–3900; tasks:1397).
- **A second starter exits 0** at once when it finds the lock held (Y:3900–3901).
  - **A failing `serve` holds the lock for a moment too.** Since round 3's N9, a `serve` that stamps a failure takes `register.lock`, without waiting, for its stamp's write (section 8.6; `lanes-index`:7611–7616 at T512's `f21ca4a7`). So a starter tries the lock five times over 100 ms, once at once and then four times 25 ms apart, and takes it for a live service's only where it is still held after the last attempt; then it exits 0 and starts nothing, and the exit codes are as before (lane 1's NIT-B, #165 6883749855; `REGISTER_SERVE_LOCK_WAITS`, `lanes-index`:9174, and the attempts, :9229–9245, at T512's `bb19b1ad`, its commit `bb19b1ad`). Each attempt takes only contention, `EAGAIN`, `EWOULDBLOCK` or `EACCES`, for the lock held, and makes an attempt a signal interrupts, `EINTR`, again at once, up to 100 times. New in draft 19, a rule lane 5 names for lane 1's U5 and lane 3's review: a `register.lock` that opens but cannot be taken for any reason but contention, any other error of `flock`, or 100 interruptions, is no live service's lock; the start writes that `register.lock` "could not be locked", with the error, to standard error and to `register.log`, and exits 69, as for a `register.lock` that cannot be opened. That differs from a held lock, which is a live service's: there the start exits 0 and starts nothing, since the service it would start is running, while a lock it cannot take for another reason is a fault of the start, which its 69 reports, and it starts nothing either. As built at T512's `1e1846e3`, its round 5 (commit `30ee202f`): `register_serve_try_lock`, `lanes-index`:9239–9256, and the start, :9338–9351. At `bb19b1ad` any failure of `flock` was taken for a held lock, so five failures of another kind, `EINTR` among them, exited 0 and started nothing (Codex's review of T512 at `bb19b1ad`, NIT 8; :9236–9245 there). A stamp's write is one small file, which normally ends inside those 100 ms, so a healthy start is no longer turned away by it. A write that held the lock past them would still turn one away, and its fresh stamp would hold starts back for 60 s (section 8.6); the contract accepts that as a limit, since it needs two `serve`s that disagree and a write that stalls. At `f21ca4a7` the starter made one attempt (:9127–9131 there, :9115–9119 at `26c77534`), so one that landed in the write exited 0 and started nothing (lane 5's read-only review of round 3).
- **Holding the lock,** the starter tries the socket: one that answers means a live service, and it exits 0; `ECONNREFUSED` means a stale socket, which Y has it unlink before it binds and listens (Y:3901–3903). As built, it binds and listens at a temporary name of its own in `run/`, writes the owner file, and renames that socket over the stale one, which it never unlinks, so the fixed path is replaced in one step; where the name it draws is taken, it draws another, up to 8 names, and then exits 69, and it unlinks only a name this attempt bound (section 2.4, "How it is written"; `lanes-index`:9263–9271 at T512's `bb19b1ad`; :9376–9406 at `1e1846e3`, T512's round 5; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, its fourth finding, a NIT, #165 6885771975).
- **Shown** by probe B1: ten racing clients started ten starters; one bound, nine exited on the held lock, and all ten clients were answered (Y:3903–3906).

### 8.4 When it stops

- **The idle stop.** After 15 minutes, 900 s, with no request, it unlinks its socket under its lock and exits (Y:3912–3914; tasks:1399). Section 10 gives the test-only value.
  - **A service its clients cannot use stops this way.** A client that finds an owner file it cannot use, or one of another namespace or boot, sends no request (section 2.4), so such a service's idle clock runs on, and it stops at its idle stop. The next client then meets `ENOENT` and starts a service of its own (section 8.1; OP-17).
- **A service whose worker has ended** stops at once, since it can answer nothing, and the next client meets `ENOENT` and starts its own (lane 1's gate, S2, #165 6881993079; `lanes-index`:8310–8313 at T512's `26c77534`, its commit `0c4a8f1c`).
- **An install change.** It checks its own `lanes-index` file's size, mtime and inode on each request, one `realpath` and two `stat` calls, the module's failing with `ENOENT` where it is absent (one, where the launcher's own fails; `register_serve_install`, `lanes-index`:8222–8235 at T512's `4444ddc4`), and when they differ it answers `unavailable restarting` and exits (Y:3914–3918). Since follow-up 1's parts 2, 4 and 5, it checks the launcher by its real path, and T124's module beside it, on each request (section 2.4; `answer`, `lanes-index`:9117 at T512's `4e343ea2`, :9079 at `23cb3799`); its own `__file__` is the path the client invoked, with no lexical normalization (section 8.2), so a link to the launcher repointed to another launcher is a change it sees, as before, where the service was started through that link (next sub-bullet).
  - **A service of another install, running, is a v1.3 limit** (OP-20, closed: lane 3's rulings, #165 6894714082, option 1, and #165 6895062857, which takes the second live install as the same limit; lane 5's correction, #165 6894693645; lane 5's reviewer, `R62-REPORT-1115.md`, SHOULD-1; lane 1's U5 NIT 3, #165 6895008151). **Its four cases,** one mechanism: a checkout run by hand, a second live install at another real path; a `link-estates` "RELINK"; a changed `OPENREPOTOOLS_BIN_DIR`; and a link repointed above the path a service was started by. "A hand-run checkout, a `link-estates` RELINK and a changed `OPENREPOTOOLS_BIN_DIR` all meet the repointed link's mechanism" (lane 3, #165 6895062857). The check sees a repoint only where the service was started through that link. `lanes-edit.sh` starts every service by `lanes-index` in its own `readlink -f` directory: it resolves itself with `readlink -f` and takes its directory from the result (`lanes-edit.sh`:1226–1232 at T512's `4e343ea2`), and `lanes_index_bin` returns the `lanes-index` in that directory where it is executable, and falls back to `PATH` only where it is not (:6178–6184), for the `SessionStart` start (:6672–6677), the guard's prefetch (:6693–6703) and the guard read (:7109–7114) alike. Strictly, the path is that directory plus `lanes-index`; it is the real path in practice, since the installer places regular files there (lane 1's U5 NIT 4 and its install answer, #165 6895008151). So a service it starts does not see a link repointed above that path. **The consequence, the same in each case,** in lane 3's words: "A client of one install finds a service of another install running. Its own `serve` exits at the held `register.lock`, and the other service answers it with that install's code until that service stops for another cause." (#165 6895062857.) As built at `4e343ea2`: the client reads another install (`lanes-index`:9778), its `serve` exits at the lock (section 8.3), and it still sends its request (:9792), which that service answers, never `unavailable restarting`, since that service's own files have not changed; and that service never goes idle while those requests come.
