## 13. The bytes on the socket (proposal, lane 5, with lane 1's U5 review folded in)

Draft 2's answer to OP-3, with draft 3's additions and draft 4's fold of lane 1's U5 review. The sources fix what a request and an answer carry (sections 4 and 5); this section fixes how they travel. Drafts 2 and 3 marked each of OP-3's items "(proposal, lane 5, for lane 1's U5 review)"; draft 4 marks each such subsection "with lane 1's U5 review folded in", or says that the review passes it. Every value here that no source gives is marked "(proposal, lane 5)", with its reason. Where a choice touches Y, svc, CLI45, CLI46, CLI47 or one of lane 3's rulings, the bullet says which, and why it stays inside it. Lane 1 has answered OP-4 to OP-7, OP-11, OP-13 and OP-15 (#165 6746620707), and draft 3 encoded what those answers need: OP-4's cause words (13.5); OP-5's sets and listing (13.3 and 13.4); OP-6's resolution (13.4); and what forms 1 and 3 send under it (13.9).

**Lane 1's U5 review** (#165 6747151224) passes the frame: one request per connection; ASCII lines with byte-counted bodies; `text <n> <generation>`; `file <path> <sha256> <size>`, `absent` and `ambiguous`; `end` and the close; the one-line refusals; 16,777,216 bytes over the whole answer; the malformed cases; one monotonic deadline, set once the request is built, before the client's checks and its `connect()`, as section 13.8 sets it, where lane 1's review passed it as timed from `connect()`; and nothing written until the answer is whole. Wherever lane 1's review gives bytes or text for something draft 3 proposed, lane 1's replace lane 5's, since lane 1 writes `register read`'s contract (`register-cli.md`'s §3.6) and its direct path (T113), which T512's socket client shares, and reviews that client under U5 (lane 1's U5 re-check, N-d, #165 6752336451). Each such place is marked "(lane 1's U5 review, <item>)", and section 14.3 lists them. Lane 1's U5 re-check of draft 4 passes the section, and OP-3 is closed (#165 6752336451). Draft 5 folds the re-check's SHOULD and NITs, with lane 1's bytes in place of lane 5's by the same rule; each such place is marked "(lane 1's U5 re-check, <item>)", and section 14.4 lists them. Draft 6 cites lane 1's contract draft 4.11 as `CLI411:<n>` where draft 5 cited it as to come, and takes draft 4.11's words where they differ from draft 5's; section 14.5 lists them.

### 13.1 In one view

```text
The request, client to service:

read 1\n
file <path>\n                          a file asked by name; a log's <key> is any non-empty bytes of 0x21-0x7E (13.3)
set <prefix>\n                         a set: lanes/archive/ or lanes/log/ (13.3; OP-5)
list\n                                 the render set's paths present at n (13.3; OP-5)
end\n

The answer, service to client, then the service closes the connection. One of:

text <n> <generation>\n                the head line; then one item for each request line, in the request's order:
file <path> <sha256> <size>\n          for a file line: a file present at n, followed by its <size> bytes and one \n
absent <path>\n                        for a file line: a file absent at n
ambiguous <path>\n                     for a file line: a log name that two logs at n match, differing only by case (13.4; OP-6)
set <prefix> <count>\n                 for a set line: then <count> file records, one per member, in bytewise order of <path>
list <count>\n                         for the list line: then <count> lines, path <path> <sha256>\n each, in bytewise order of <path>
end\n                                  after the last item

refuse db <cause> <sqlstate>\n         <cause>: unreachable, auth or switch-files; four fields (13.5)
refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>\n
                                       <cause> schema: six fields (13.5)

unavailable\n
unavailable slow\n
unavailable restarting\n
```

### 13.2 What the format must meet

- **The cache family, unchanged.** The client writes from an answer what the direct read writes from its own snapshot: `.head`'s `n=`, `generation=`, `file=` and `absent=` rows, the files, and `.paths`, from the list, where form 1 asks for it or where form 3's family holds it or `--files` names it (CLI45:655–704; CLI47:665; CLI46:680; 13.4); form 3's `list` on every call is its digest source and makes `.paths` no member (13.9), as T113 writes `.paths` only as a member (`lanes-index`:6261–6263, :7044 and :7093 at line R's merge95; lane 1's confirmation of drafts 10 and 11, NIT-d, opensoft/openRepoTools-spec#46 6867197476). `.head` has a `file=` row for every path read at `n`, present or not, and one for each file of a set member (CLI47:699, CLI47:703). So an answer carries the database's values only: `n`, the generation, and, for each file asked, its path at `n`, whether it is present there, its digest and its bytes; for a set, the same for each of its members; and for the list, the render set's paths present at `n`, each with its digest there. `.head`'s `c=`, `n_a=` and `w_s_ms=` come from the client's own configuration, as the direct read takes them (CLI45:296–303).
- **No byte added or lost.** A file's bytes travel behind their count, never escaped, quoted or re-encoded. The client never decodes them as text: it hands them to the writer that the direct read hands its own bytes to (T113's). So each cache file holds the same bytes whichever path read it, which is what lane 2's test 10 compares through the guard's output (svc:86–90; tp:1912–1916).
- **Standard-library Python at both ends.** `lanes-index serve`, and `register read` as its client (T512), need `socket`, `select` and `hashlib`, among the modules SVC-3 names (Y:3854–3857). ASCII lines and decimal counts need no `struct` and no third-party framing.
- **One round trip a request.** An answer holds nothing that the request's one transaction, sent as one query, does not read (Y:3985–3988; Y:4038–4041).
- **Small.** ASCII lines that end in `\n`, a byte count before each body, and one end line.

### 13.3 The request (proposal, lane 5, with lane 1's U5 review folded in)

- **One request per connection, said outright** (proposal, lane 5). The client connects, sends one request and reads one answer; the service answers it and closes the connection. Reason: Y's wait runs "connect and answer together" (Y:4182) and is timed "from the `connect()` call to the last byte" (Y:4393), Y's older wording, which section 13.8's deadline, set before the checks and the connect, supersedes; and the close is how the client knows the answer has ended (13.4).
- **Its form, and how it ends** (proposal, lane 5): the line `read 1`, the request's kind and this format's version; one line for each thing the client wants, in the order it wants the answer's items: `file <path>` for a file it names, `set <prefix>` for a set, and `list` for the render set's listing; then the line `end`. Each line ends in one `\n` (0x0A), with no `\r`, and one space (0x20) separates its fields. Reason: both ends are one install, since a client starts a new service when the owner file names another install (Y:3881–3883) and the old one answers `unavailable restarting` where its own files changed (section 8.4's limit; OP-20) (Y:3915–3918). So the version only guards the moment between an upgrade and the old service's exit, for seven bytes, and, in section 8.4's limit, the time until the other install's service stops.
- **Each line once, in one order** (the Codex review, SHOULD 2; as T512 builds it, `lanes-index`:6531–6545 and :8992–9060 at line R's merge106). A client sends no line twice. It reads `--files` as a set, so a member named twice, as in `--files register,register`, is one member; and form 3's members and keys are the union of the cache's, `--files`'s, `--log-key=`'s and `.head`'s `absent=` keys, each once. Its lines go in this order, each where the form sends it (13.9): `file lanes/LANES.md`, `list`, `set lanes/archive/`, `file lanes/aliases.tsv`, form 1's `set lanes/log/`, then one `file lanes/log/<key>.md` for each key, in bytewise order of the key; form 2's go `file lanes/LANES.md`, `file lanes/aliases.tsv`, then its log.
  - **Keys are bytes.** Two keys that differ only by case are two keys, and go on two lines. The service resolves each, both may name one path, and each is answered by its own record, so each key keeps its own `.log.<key>` and `.logpath.<key>` (CLI45:683–684; 13.4).
  - **The service takes any order.** It answers each line in the request's order (13.4), and a line a request repeats is answered again, in full, within 13.4's bound on the whole answer. The service plans each distinct line once: a repeated line, a `list` or `set` line or a `file` line's name, reuses the plan its first line made, before the answer is counted and outside the request's timer, and each line is still answered, in order, and counted as it is built (lane 1's N8, #165 6881993079; `text`'s plan, `lanes-index`:8855–8864, and `plan_line`, :8904–8925, at T512's `bb19b1ad`, its commit `93e936ff`). At `26c77534` and `f21ca4a7` each repeated line was planned again.
- **Its encoding** (proposal, lane 5): ASCII. Every name is bytes 0x21 to 0x7E, so no name is quoted or escaped. Reason: every name a client sends is in that range (next bullet).
- **The names** (lane 1's U5 review, M1, in place of draft 3's key rule): `lanes/LANES.md`, `lanes/aliases.tsv`, and `lanes/log/<key>.md`, where `<key>` is any non-empty key whose bytes are all 0x21 to 0x7E, in `check_lane_name`'s class or not, `/` and `,` included.
  - **Names are byte strings.** The service compares names as byte strings, folding A–Z only, and never as file-system paths. So `lanes/log/../LANES.md` resolves to no log, and answers `absent`.
  - **Who sends which key.** Forms 1 and 2 name a log only for a `<key>` in `check_lane_name`'s class, `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, not opening with `.` or `-`, and skip a key outside it (Y:4956; CLI46:670). Form 3 also names a `--log-key=` key outside the class (CLI47:730), and a key outside the class may hold `/` (CLI46:670). Form 4 never asks (B7).
  - **A key with any other byte is not sent:** a space, a control byte or a byte above 0x7E. Only form 3 can hold such a key, and that form-3 call reads directly, the whole call, so that its one snapshot stays one (Y:4890; 13.9). Reason, lane 1's: reading directly is narrower than a hex line, whose resolved path would then need an encoding too.
- **Sets and the listing** (lane 1's U5 review, M1, under lane 3's ruling, #165 6747048305, ERR-31): `set lanes/archive/` and `set lanes/log/`, those two only, each prefix byte for byte, ending in `/`; and the line `list`, with no field, in place of draft 3's `listing`. These are lane 1's three: `archive` is the set `lanes/archive/`; `--all`'s logs are the set `lanes/log/`; `paths` is the render set's listing at `n` (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-5; CLI47:665). A set covers every path of the render set present at `n` that begins with its prefix. The render set is `lanes/LANES.md`, `lanes/aliases.tsv`, each `lanes/log/<name>.md` but `lanes/log/README.md`, and each `lanes/archive/<name>.md` (Y:258–259; `lanes-index`:1937 and :1969–1972 at line R's merge106), and the list names every path of it present at `n`, the two single files included (13.4; the Codex review, BLOCKING 2). The paths under the two prefixes are `lanes/archive/<name>.md` and `lanes/log/<name>.md`, with no `/` in `<name>` (Y:1145–1146, Y:1170–1171), so a set has no subdirectory.
- **The version stays `read 1`** (lane 1's U5 review, 1(c)). No service has shipped (Y:4868), so the `set` and `list` lines belong in `read 1` from the start, as draft 3 already put them, not under a new version, as draft 2 planned. The service answers only `read 1`; a future format bumps to `read 2`, and a service answers any version it does not speak with `unavailable` (below). A mismatch then fails as `unavailable`, the safe outcome, instead of a misparse. Draft 2's reason for the version line stands (above), and `read 1` also guards the case where the owner file names another install (#165 6747151224).
- **File names only, inside B7.** The `read 1` and `end` lines frame the request and carry no fact. A set or the list names render-set files by their paths, never a host-local fact or an `n` (CLI47:665), and lane 3's ruling lets a reader's request name them: "the files it wants, singly or by the contract's member sets, or the render set's listing at `n`" (#165 6747048305; ERR-31, on Y:4036–4037). So a request carries nothing but the names of the files the client wants: no `n`, no generation, no session id, `cwd`, agent id, prompt, window, live record or liveness fact (Y:4036–4037; svc:59, svc:61–63; Y:4611; B7). That is what the recording stand-in of `test_the_guards_request_carries_only_file_names` reads (tp:2045–2047).
- **Its size limit** (proposal, lane 5): 65,536 bytes, the `end` line included. Reason: the largest request a client sends names the register, the aliases and one log for each lane, about 3 KB for a register of 73 lanes, the real one's shape (tp:1903–1906). The limit is about twenty times that, and bounds what a stray connection can make the service hold. With sets, a form-3 request names `lanes/archive/` as its set, not each file in it (lane 1's U5 review, M1), each cached log on its own `file` line, and `list`, and never `set lanes/log/` (13.9).
  - **A request over the bound is not sent** (lane 1's U5 review, N5). A client whose request, its `end` line included, would pass 65,536 bytes reads directly, by its own decision, and writes no `.direct`, since the service is not at fault (section 11.3).
- **How long the service waits for it** (proposal, lane 5): from the accept to the `end` line, at most the client's own wait, `w_s`: 300 ms, or `register_rtt_ms=` plus 200 ms where that is over 100 ms (CLI44:298–301; Y:4182). The service computes it from the key it already reads for its timer (CLI45:278). At that wait it closes the connection with no answer. Reason: a client that has not sent its whole request by then has stopped waiting (Y:4182), so an answer would reach no one. It leaves SVC-6's per-request timer as it is (next bullet).
- **Where the service stops reading** (the Codex review, NIT 1; as T512 builds it, `lanes-index`:8102–8113 and :8695–8744 at line R's merge106). It reads up to the request's first `end` line, and no further. A stream that ends before that line, the client's close or a reset among them, gets no answer, and nothing is written to `register.log`, as for a request still short of its `end` line at the wait above. Bytes after `end\n` are never answered: those that came with the request are passed over, and no more is read. A request that passes 65,536 bytes before its `end` line is one the service cannot take (below).
- **The request's arrival** is the moment the service has read the `end` line. The per-request timer, 200 ms, or `register_rtt_ms=` plus 100 ms where that is over 100 ms (Y:4001–4002), runs from there, as AT-10 reads `unavailable slow` "no earlier than 200 ms after the request reached it" (tp:1966–1970).
- **A connection that sends no request** gets no answer (proposal, lane 5). The service closes it when the client closes, or at the wait above. It is not a request, so it does not move the idle stop's clock, which counts time "with no request" (Y:3912). Reason: a try that only connects, such as the starter's under the lock, where "one that answers means a live service" (Y:3901–3903), then costs the service nothing. There Y:3902's "answers" is the accepted `connect()`, so the starter's try sends no request (lane 1's U5 review, N7). `serve --start` makes no try of its own (section 9).
- **A request the service cannot take** (proposal, lane 5): a first line other than `read 1`; a line that is none of `file <path>`, `set <prefix>`, `list` and `end`; a name outside the three shapes, an empty `<key>` included; a prefix other than the two; no line between `read 1` and `end`; or more than 65,536 bytes, its `end` line included. The service answers `unavailable` (13.5), and writes the fixed phrase `bad request` to `register.log`, never the request's text. Reason: the client then reads directly, as for any `unavailable`, and the log keeps to fixed phrases, as Y:3797–3799 has it.

### 13.4 The text at `n` (proposal, lane 5, with lane 1's U5 review folded in)

- **The head line, with `n` and the generation** (proposal, lane 5, for OP-2; lane 1's U5 review passes it): `text <n> <generation>`.
  - `<n>` is the head the request's transaction read, in ASCII decimal, with no sign: `0`, or a number with no leading zero, as `<size>` is written (lane 1's U5 review, N1). Lane 2's fixture can stand at an empty journal.
  - `<generation>` is the register generation the same transaction read, as PostgreSQL prints a `uuid`: 36 characters, lowercase hex in groups of 8, 4, 4, 4 and 12, joined by `-`.
  - Reason: Y's head, `text <n>` (Y:4064–4066), stays the line's opening, and the generation joins it on the same line because it comes from the same snapshot (Y:4833) and goes to `.head`'s `generation=` beside `n=` (CLI45:694–695). It touches Y:4064–4066, which names no generation, and stays inside Y by adding only what Y:4833 requires. Lane 3's ruling makes that an erratum on Y:4064–4066, ERR-26, whose head reads "`text <n>` with the register generation that transaction read" (#165 6746215510, OP-2); the bytes are this proposal, which lane 1's U5 review passes.
- **Every decimal field is 1 to 18 digits** (the Codex re-review, S4; as built, `lanes-index`:9032 at T512's `88b50886`, :8966 at merge106). `<n>`, `<size>`, a set's or the list's `<count>`, and `refuse db schema`'s `<readers_from>` and `<version>` are each `0`, or a number with no leading zero, of at most 18 digits, so below 10^18. A field of 19 digits or more is out of its form, so the answer is malformed, `unavailable`, and the direct read decides (13.7). `<n>` is the journal's head, a PostgreSQL `bigint`, which passes 18 digits only after 10^18 entries; `<size>` is bounded by 16,777,216 bytes in any case (13.6); and the counts and the schema's numbers stay far below the cap.
- **One item for each request line, in the request's order** (proposal, lane 5). The i-th item answers the request's i-th line between `read 1` and `end`: one record for a `file` line, a set's head line and its members for a `set` line, and the list's head line and its `path` lines for the `list` line. Reason: the client knows from its own request how many items come and what each answers, so no index travels; a set's or the list's own count says where it ends (below).
- **A file present at `n`** (proposal, lane 5): the line `file <path> <sha256> <size>`, then exactly `<size>` bytes, the file's body, then one `\n`, which is not part of the file.
  - `<path>` is the file's path at `n`: the name asked; for a log, the path it resolved to; for a set's member, its own path (below).
  - `<sha256>` is the database's digest of the file at `n` (Y:4070–4072), the value `.head`'s `file=` row carries (CLI47:699), written as §3.2's canonical row writes it: 64 lowercase hex (CLI45:452–456).
  - `<size>` is the body's length in bytes, in ASCII decimal, with no sign and no leading zero, and `0` for an empty file.
  - Reason for the `\n` after the body: "a byte count that does not match" (Y:4076–4077) then fails at the next byte, before the digest is computed.
  - The client writes `.head`'s `file=` row for it from `<path>`, `1` and `<sha256>` (CLI47:699).
- **How a file absent at `n` is marked** (proposal, lane 5): the line `absent <path>`, with `<path>` the name asked, byte for byte. Reason: the client needs only the fact, and the name ties it to its request. From it the client writes what the direct read writes for a member absent at `n`, so that `.head` and the files are the same both ways (CLI45 §3.6):
  - for `lanes/aliases.tsv`, `.aliases` as the one line `# no lane aliases` (Y:4150; CLI45:682), and the `file=` row `0` and `-` that contract draft 4.7 gives a path absent at `n` (CLI47:699, CLI47:703; CLI45:453–454), which draft 3 left to "whatever `file=` row the direct read writes" (lane 1's U5 review, S3);
  - for a log, an empty `.log.<key>` and `.logpath.<key>`, and the row `absent=<key>` in place of a `file=` row, since no path names it (CLI45:683–684, CLI45:700; CLI47:703).
- **A log name** (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-6; CLI46:673; the records' bytes are lane 5's proposal, which lane 1's U5 review passes). The request names `lanes/log/<key>.md` in the key's own spelling, and the service resolves it as the direct read does: case-insensitively among the render set's paths at its `n`, in the request's own transaction, by T113's one function, which `serve` reuses (SVC-3; CLI46:673; Y:4957). That function compares bytes and folds A–Z only, as Python's `bytes.lower()` does, for every key, in the class or outside it (lane 1's U5 review, 1(a)). Form 2 caches no `.paths` (CLI45:680), so the client cannot resolve the name itself. The answer:
  - one log resolves: a `file` record whose `<path>` is the resolved `lanes/log/<File>.md`, which may differ from the name asked in case. A resolved path in another case answers the name asked, and is not "a file it did not ask for" (CLI46:673; Y:4077). The client writes `.log.<key>` from the body, and `.logpath.<key>` as that `<path>` (CLI45:683–684);
  - none resolves: `absent lanes/log/<key>.md`, and the client writes both files empty, with an `absent=<key>` row (CLI46:673);
  - two or more differ from `<key>` only by case: the line `ambiguous lanes/log/<key>.md`, with the name asked. Forms 1 and 2 write neither `.log.<key>` nor `.logpath.<key>`, as the direct read writes neither, and form 3 exits 69 for a key the call was asked for, and drops from the family a cached member that turns ambiguous (C11; CLI46:673, CLI45:683; lane 1's U5 review, 1(a); lane 1's U5 re-check, N-g, as lane 3 accepted it, #165 6752360829; 13.9).
  - It stays inside D17: resolving a path decides nothing, and it is the resolution the direct read makes, in the same `lanes-index` (Y:3835–3838). Both ends resolve alike because they run one function (CLI46:673), and lane 1 has decided that it folds A–Z only (lane 1's U5 review, 1(a)), which answers draft 2's question.
  - `ambiguous` stays a record of its own, as lane 1's answer has it: the answer "marks it as matching two logs" (CLI46:673). The service never answers the whole request `unavailable` for it. That closes the choice draft 2 left open, in `ambiguous`'s favour, and lane 1's U5 review takes it: two logs that differ only by case stand until a hand merge ends them, and a whole-request `unavailable` would cost every prompt a direct read, a `.direct` flag and a fallback line for as long as they stand (lane 1's U5 review, 1(a)). An `ambiguous` record for a name that is not a log is malformed (13.7).
- **A set** (lane 1's U5 review, M1, which draft 3's proposal already met): the line `set <prefix> <count>`, then `<count>` `file` records, one for each member, in bytewise order of `<path>`, as `LC_ALL=C` sorts, none repeated.
  - `<prefix>` is the request's prefix, byte for byte. `<count>` is the number of members, in ASCII decimal, with no sign: `0`, or a number with no leading zero (lane 1's U5 re-check, N-c).
  - Each member is a path of the render set present at `n` that begins with `<prefix>`, read in the request's own transaction (CLI47:665; Y:4036–4041), and its record is a `file` record as above: its path, its digest at `n`, its size, then its body and one `\n`. A set holds no `absent` or `ambiguous` record: it lists what is present.
  - **An empty set** is the line `set <prefix> 0`, with no record after it.
  - The client writes `.head`'s `file=` rows for a set member from its records: one `file=` row for every path the set's answer names, its text cached or not, from its `<path>`, `1` and `<sha256>`, as the direct read does, where a set member "gives one row per file it holds at `n`, its text cached or not" (CLI47:699, CLI411:703; lane 1's U5 re-check, S-a, #165 6752336451). A log that `--all` drops from the class, or one of two that differ only by case (C11), keeps its row with no cached text (CLI411:703), and form 3 needs a digest at `n′` for that row, as for every other (13.9). T113 builds the same rows on the direct path (#165 6752336451). This is form 1's `--all`. Under form 3, `--all`'s logs are no member, and form 3 never sends `set lanes/log/`: it takes every row's digest at `n′` from the list (below), and carries the rows of logs `.head` already had that are present at `n′`, at their digests at `n′` (13.9; lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, S-b, #165 6828035829; lane 1's U5 review of draft 9, S-c, #165 6833882556; lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772).
  - **`lanes/archive/`:** the client hands every member to the writer the direct read uses for `.archive`, which holds "`lanes/archive/*`, as `archive_text` prints it today" (CLI46:681).
  - **`lanes/log/`, filtered to the class, in form 1:** the client keeps a member `lanes/log/<File>.md` only where `<File>` is in `check_lane_name`'s class, tested without dying (Y:4956), and takes it as `--all`'s member `log:<File>` (CLI47:665, which keeps this filter: "of which the client keeps the names in the class"). It resolves `<File>` among the set's paths by T113's one function, as a named log is resolved, and writes `.log.<File>` and `.logpath.<File>` as the direct read writes them, or neither where two differ only by case (C11; CLI46:673, CLI46:683–684). A member outside the class it drops: it writes no file for it, but writes its `file=` row, as every member has one (above; lane 1's U5 re-check, S-a), and checks its record like any other (13.7). The render set never holds `lanes/log/README.md` (Y:258–259; Y:1145–1146). Form 3 never sends `set lanes/log/`: a log's text comes from a `file` line, for a cached `log:<key>`, or from the first read that names `log:<key>` (B19-cli:733; 13.9; S-b).
  - Reason: the count says where the set ends with no closing line of its own, so a cut set is caught as a cut answer is; bytewise order is `.paths`' own order (CLI46:680), and lets the client check that each member follows the last; and each member travels as a named file does, so the client writes it with the same code and the same checks.
- **The list** (lane 1's U5 review, M1, in place of draft 3's listing lines; each path's digest, lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772, by lane 5's decision): the line `list <count>`, then `<count>` lines `path <path> <sha256>`, each one path of the render set present at `n` with that file's digest at `n`, in bytewise order of `<path>`, none repeated. Its paths are `.paths` (CLI46:680). `<count>` is written as a set's is: `0`, or a number with no leading zero (lane 1's U5 re-check, N-c).
  - `<sha256>` is written as a `file` record's is: the database's digest of the file at `n` (Y:4070–4072), 64 lowercase hex, the value a `.head` `file=` row carries (CLI47:699). The fields come in a `file` record's order, the path, then the digest.
  - **Why the list carries a digest.** Form 3 tests every `.head` `file=` row by its path's digest at `n′`, as T113 tests its rows against the listing it reads on every call, each path with its digest, straight from SQL and with no body (`lanes-index`:6086, :7046–7074 at line R's merge30). So form 3 takes every row's digest from the list, at one digest per path, and never needs `set lanes/log/` for digests (13.9; lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772).
  - **The shape is this contract's own.** Y's SVC-7 gives a file's answer as its path, its byte count, its digest and its bytes, and defines no listing (Y:4034–4067). The `list` line and its answer are this contract's (lane 1's U5 review, M1, under lane 3's ERR-31, which rules on what a request may name, not on the answer's shape), and lane 1's contract leaves "how a set and a listing are encoded" to it (B19-cli:666). So the digest is lane 5's to add, and needs no ruling of lane 3's (#165 6834158772; #165 6834196887).
  - **An empty list** is the line `list 0`, with no line after it.
  - The render set does not hold `lanes/STORE` (Y:258–260; Y:5050; CLI411:680), so neither does the list.
  - The client writes `.paths` from the `path` lines' paths only, "one per line, sorted bytewise (`LC_ALL=C`)" (CLI46:680), and never their digests, and only where `.paths` is a member: form 1's `paths`, or, in form 3, where the family holds `.paths` or `--files` names it (13.2; 13.9). A form-3 `list` that is no member writes no `.paths` (the Codex review, SHOULD 1; `lanes-index`:6261–6263 and :7044 at line R's merge106).
  - The list carries no text, so it takes no body, and its paths, in `.paths`' order, are what `.paths` holds. The client cannot check a `path` line's digest against a body, as it checks a `file` record's (13.7). It takes it as it takes the head line's `n`, from a service whose trust rests on SVC-2 (Y:4070–4076; section 3).
- **A path the lines cannot carry** (lane 1's U5 review, M1, with draft 3's reason). Every path an answer names is bytes 0x21 to 0x7E, as every line but a body is (13.7). The render set's names may hold any byte but `/` (Y:1145–1146, Y:1170–1171). So where a path the answer would carry, in a set or the list, has a byte outside 0x21 to 0x7E, the service answers the whole request `unavailable` (13.5), writes the fixed phrase `bad name` to `register.log`, and the client reads directly. A resolved log's path differs from the name asked only in A–Z case, so it always fits. Reason: the lines stay ASCII and split on single spaces. Such a name costs a direct read, which then decides, never a wrong cache, and paths made by lane tooling are in the class, so this is a guard, not a path anyone takes (lane 1's U5 review, M1).
- **One answer, one view of each path** (the Codex review, BLOCKING 3). An answer can name one path in more than one item: a `file` record, a set's member, and a `path` line of the list. Every item comes from the request's one transaction, which reads one digest for each path at `n` (Y:4064–4065), so the items agree:
  - **one digest a path:** every item that names a path names it with the same digest;
  - **the list covers the rest:** where the answer holds the list, every path a `file` record or a set's member names is one of its paths, and each set's members are exactly its paths that begin with the set's prefix;
  - **a name's record agrees with the paths:** against the list, where the answer holds it, and against each set whose prefix the name begins with, each on its own and never their union, an `absent` record's name matches none of its paths, an `ambiguous` record's name matches two or more of them, and a `file` record's path is the one path among them its name matches. A name matches a path byte for byte, or, for a log, by T113's one function (the log name, above).
  - **named records agree among themselves,** with a list or a set or without one (the Codex re-review, B4; a requirement ahead of the code, as lane 3 confirmed it, #165 6880543448): two `file`, `absent` or `ambiguous` records whose names match the same paths, being the same name byte for byte, or two log names that are equal once A–Z is folded, as T113's one function folds them, are the same kind of record, and two `file` records among them name the same path with the same digest. So, for a request that names `lanes/log/foo.md` and `lanes/log/FOO.md`, an answer with `file lanes/log/Foo.md …` for one and `absent lanes/log/FOO.md` for the other is a mismatch, though it holds no list and no set.
  - **repeated views agree** (new in draft 19, a rule lane 5 names for lane 1's U5 and lane 3's review): two sets of one prefix in one answer name the same paths, as two lists in one answer do, and the client refuses an answer where either differs as a mismatch. Both rest on one view of each path, since the request's one transaction gives each path one view, and a correct service never sends either. The lists' half states what T512's client already did (`lanes-index`:9781–9783 at T512's `bb19b1ad`; :9918–9924 at `1e1846e3`); the sets' half is new in T512's round 5, as built at `1e1846e3` (:9913–9917, its commit `0cf57988`).
  - The service builds every item from that transaction's one listing, so its answers always agree (`lanes-index`:8595–8650 at line R's merge106). An answer whose items disagree is "a mismatch" (Y:4076): the client discards all of it, writes no cache file and reads directly, as for any malformed answer (Y:4076–4079; 13.7). So form 3 never writes a body with another item's digest, and never marks absent a path the list holds. T512's client checks all four, as built at T512's `26c77534` (`register_serve_agree`, `lanes-index`:9601 and :9606–9687): the fourth by grouping the named records by name, a log's name folded A–Z, each group of one kind, one path and one digest (:9633–9639, its commit `b93c5a3f`); and the third against each covering view on its own, each view's logs folded once (:9668–9686, its commit `97746b38`). As built at T512's `1e1846e3`, its round 5 (commit `0cf57988`) brings the answer to its distinct views and names first, and checks each distinct name once, and each distinct set prefix once, against the list by the range of the list's sorted paths that begin with the prefix, finding the sets that cover a name by a trie of the prefixes (`register_serve_agree`, `lanes-index`:9858–9973, its names at :9901–9908, its sets against the list at :9929–9936, with `_register_prefix_range`, :9976–9985, and its trie at :9941–9972). At `bb19b1ad` it held each set against the whole list, and each named record against each covering view, so on a valid answer within the bounds its time could grow as records times views (Codex's review of T512 at `bb19b1ad`, SHOULD 4; `lanes-index`:9784–9813 there). The rules are unchanged but for the repeated views, above. That per-view check is defense in depth: the client sends no nested and no repeated set prefix (13.3), so no answer to a request it sends can reach the fault a union of views would have, and round 2's list-and-set rule already refuses the cases it can send; the per-view check holds every covering view to the rule regardless. At `88b50886` it checked the first three (:9341 and :9346–9401), the third against the union of the covering views, and skipped a named record that no list or set covered (:9392–9393).
- **How an answer ends** (proposal, lane 5): the line `end` after the last item; then the service closes the connection. The client takes an answer as whole only where it has read the head line, one item for each request line, the line `end`, and then the end of the stream, with no byte after `end\n`. Reason: a cut answer then always lacks its end line or its close, so it is never taken for a whole one, and the digests still catch a corrupted body (Y:4072–4074).
- **Built whole, then sent** (proposal, lane 5). The service writes no byte of a text answer until its transaction has answered and every body has been checked against its digest at `n` (Y:4043–4050). Reason: its timer then ends a request with `unavailable slow` (13.5), never with half a text answer, and no answer mixes two seqs (Y:4064–4065).
  - **Counted as it is built** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879724890, point 3; the Codex review, SHOULD 4; as built, `lanes-index`:8012–8016 and :8646–8714 at T512's `88b50886`). The service counts the text answer record by record and line by line as it builds it, from its head line to `end`. At the first record or line that would carry it past 16,777,216 bytes, the bound the client reads to (13.6), it stops, builds nothing more, sends `unavailable` in its place, and writes the fixed phrase `an answer over 16 MB: unavailable` to `register.log`. A record's body is not copied as it is counted, and the parts are joined once, at the end. The images the service brings to `n` for the request's files and sets (section 5.1) are read before the count, and are not counted: each is one file of the render set, held once however many lines name it, so together they are bounded by the register's own size.
- **Its send deadline** (the Codex re-review, S2; as built, `lanes-index`:8785–8786 at T512's `88b50886`, :8721–8722 at merge106). Once the answer is whole, the service sends it under one deadline, `w_s` from the start of the send, on a monotonic clock, for the whole answer and not for each write. At that deadline, or at any error, it stops sending and closes the connection, and the client meets a cut answer, which is `unavailable` (13.7). A client still reading by then has passed its own wait, which began once its request was built, before its checks and its `connect()` (13.8).

### 13.5 `refuse db` and `unavailable` (proposal, lane 5, with lane 1's U5 review folded in)

- **Each is one line, then the service closes the connection** (proposal, lane 5). The client takes one only where the line ends in `\n` and no byte follows it. Reason: a cut or padded refusal then reads as malformed, which is `unavailable` (13.7), so a cut never turns into a refusal.
- **`refuse db`** (lane 1's U5 review, M2 and 1(b), #165 6747151224, in place of draft 3's optional fifth field), in two forms:
  - **`refuse db <cause> <sqlstate>`,** exactly four fields, where `<cause>` is `unreachable`, `auth` or `switch-files`;
  - **`refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>`,** exactly six fields, where `<cause>` is `schema`. The SQLSTATE field is `-`, since the window is a check on values read (Y:2334), and each number is ASCII decimal: `0`, or a number with no leading zero.
  - **`<cause>`** is one fixed word, never `psql`'s message text (Y:3797–3799, Y:4082–4085), and only these four: `unreachable`, `auth`, `switch-files` and `schema` (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-4; CLI47:767). Any other word, or a field count that does not match its cause, is malformed, so `unavailable` (13.7). Both ends are one install, so no unknown word can arrive except from a fault.
  - **`<sqlstate>`** is the five-character SQLSTATE (Y:4085), of `[0-9A-Z]`, or `-` where the refusal has none, as for a check the transaction makes on a value it read (proposal, lane 5). Reason: `-` is what §3.2's canonical row writes for a value that is not there (CLI45:454). It stays inside Y:4085, which gives the SQLSTATE where a refusal has one. Lane 1's answer gives the same `-` for the client's line (CLI47:767). A switch other than `primary` is `refuse db switch-files -` (section 5.1).
  - **Why `schema` carries the two numbers.** A version outside the window "names both numbers and the remedy, 'upgrade the tools' or 'the database is behind: run the bootstrap'" (Y:2338), and after `refuse db` "the client then refuses exactly as the direct read would" (Y:4085–4086). The client knows its own `REGISTER_SCHEMA_VERSION`, and the service's is the same install (Y:3914–3918). But which remedy applies depends on which bound failed, and only the database's `readers_from` and `version` tell (lane 1's U5 review, 1(b)).
  - **Inside Y:3797–3799.** Its "fixed phrases and the SQLSTATE only, never `psql`'s message text" bounds message text, and two integers the database gives are not `psql`'s message text. That is lane 1's reading, and lane 3 reads it the same way, so `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>` stands (#165 6747151224; #165 6747313608, item 3).
  - **What the client prints from it** is CLI47:767's second line, after the cause line `lanes-index: refuse db schema -` (section 5.2).
- **The three `unavailable` lines,** exactly:
  - `unavailable`: the server's `lock_timeout` or `statement_timeout`, and any other timeout but the per-request timer's (Y:4017–4021, Y:4086–4087); a request before `psql` has connected (Y:4014–4016); a cache miss (Y:4046–4049); a request that arrives during the background whole-file fetch (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-15; section 7); a cause the request itself did not see (section 5.2); a request the service cannot take, a version other than `read 1` included (13.3); an answer that would name a path the lines cannot carry, with `bad name` in `register.log` (13.4); a lost child, a failed statement of any SQLSTATE, and rows the service cannot read (sections 5.2 and 8.4); a request queued or running under an earlier configuration epoch, or running when another request has seen a change the worker has not yet taken (section 8.4; lane 1's U5 NIT-f, #165 6881937840); and an answer that would pass 16,777,216 bytes (13.4);
  - `unavailable slow`: the per-request timer (Y:4012–4013), for the request whose query `psql` is running and for one that expires while still queued, which is dropped from the queue with no `SIGINT` (section 6.2; lane 1's U5 review, S1); T512's round 5 answers a queued request at its own expiry, as built at `1e1846e3`, where T512 at `bb19b1ad` did not while the worker ran an earlier job (section 6.2);
  - `unavailable restarting`: an install change (Y:3916–3918), or a configuration change that ends the service, within section 8.4's two limits (section 8.4, "A change that ends it"; lane 1's U5 NIT-p, #165 6885309342).
  - The client treats the three alike: it discards the answer and reads directly (Y:4079; Y:4087–4088). The word after `unavailable` is for the record, and for AT-10, which reads `unavailable slow` (tp:1966–1970).

### 13.6 What 16 MB counts (proposal, lane 5; lane 1's U5 review passes it)

- **Every byte the client reads from the socket for one answer:** the head line, each record's line, each body and the `\n` after it, each set's and the list's head line, the list's `path` lines with their digests, and the end line; or a refusal's one line (proposal, lane 5). Reason: the bound guards the client's memory and its share of the 5 s, which every byte costs, and one count is one comparison. The lines are small beside a 2,590,927-byte `lanes/LANES.md` (Y:4050): a `path` line is its path and a 64-hex digest, about a hundred bytes, so counting them changes no real answer.
- **Its unit: 16·2^20 bytes, 16,777,216** (proposal, lane 5). Reason: the client counts bytes, a buffer's megabyte is usually 2^20 bytes, and the choice moves only lane 2's boundary case, which then has one number. It stays inside Y:4079–4080, which says "16 MB" and names no unit.
- **How the client applies it:** it holds at most 16,777,216 bytes of an answer. An answer longer than that is over 16 MB, so `unavailable` (Y:4076–4079): the client stops at the first byte past the bound, or sooner, at a record line whose `<size>` would carry the answer past it. It also stops at its wait, whichever comes first (Y:4079–4080). A large `--all` answer past the bound is `unavailable`, and the call reads directly (Y:4076–4079; lane 1's U5 review, M1).

### 13.7 Every malformed case, each `unavailable`

Y:4076–4079 makes "a mismatch or a malformed answer (a bad header, a byte count that does not match, a digest that does not match, a file it did not ask for, an answer over 16 MB, or one not complete within the 300 ms share)" `unavailable`: the client discards all of it and reads directly. Under this format, these are the cases the client meets, each under Y's words (proposal, lane 5, for the cases and their mapping, which lane 1's U5 review passes; the rows for sets and the `list` line follow lane 1's M1, and are marked so):

| The client meets | Y's words |
|---|---|
| no byte before the end of the stream, or a reset | not complete |
| a first line that is none of the head line, a `refuse db` line and the three `unavailable` lines | a bad header |
| a head line whose `<n>` has a sign, a leading zero before another digit, or a byte that is not a digit, or whose `<generation>` is not 36 lowercase characters in the uuid's groups | a bad header |
| a `refuse db` line whose `<cause>` is not one of the four, or whose field count does not match its cause: four for `unreachable`, `auth` and `switch-files`, six for `schema`; or a field out of its form: a `<sqlstate>` that is neither five of `[0-9A-Z]` nor `-`, a `schema` line whose SQLSTATE field is not `-`, or a number with a sign, a leading zero before another digit, or a byte that is not a digit (lane 1's U5 review, M2 and 1(b)) | a bad header |
| in any line but a body, a byte before its `\n` that is outside 0x20 to 0x7E, a `\r` among them | a bad header |
| any byte after the `\n` of a `refuse db` or `unavailable` line | a malformed answer |
| a record line that is not `file`, `absent` or `ambiguous`, or a field out of its form: a `<sha256>` that is not 64 lowercase hex, or a `<size>` with a sign, a leading zero before another digit, or a byte that is not a digit (lane 1's U5 re-check agrees with this narrowing, #165 6752336451) | a bad header |
| a record whose `<path>` is not the name asked at its place; for a log, one that the name asked does not match case-insensitively, by T113's one function, which folds A–Z only (CLI46:673; lane 1's U5 review, 1(a)); or an `ambiguous` record for a name that is not a log | a file it did not ask for |
| at a `set` line's place, a line that is not `set <prefix> <count>`, or a `<count>` out of its form; at the `list` line's place, a line that is not `list <count>`, or a `<count>` out of its form | a bad header |
| at a `set` line's place, a `set` line whose prefix is not the one asked there, byte for byte (lane 1's U5 review, M1) | a file it did not ask for |
| inside a set, an `absent` or `ambiguous` record; a member whose `<path>` is outside the set's prefix, is not a render-set path (Y:1145–1146), is out of bytewise order, or repeats the member before it (lane 1's U5 review, M1; lane 1's U5 re-check agrees with the narrowing to `absent` or `ambiguous`, #165 6752336451) | a file it did not ask for |
| inside the list, a line that is none of `path <path> <sha256>`, `end`, `set …` and `list …` (lane 1's U5 re-check, N-b), or a `path` line out of its form: not exactly three fields, or a `<sha256>` that is not 64 lowercase hex (draft 11, on lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772) | a bad header |
| a `path` line whose path is not a render-set path, or is `lanes/STORE`, or is out of bytewise order, or repeats the line before it (lane 1's U5 review, M1) | a file it did not ask for |
| a record or a `path` line where the next item, or `end`, belongs: more records than a set's `<count>`, more `path` lines than the list's, or any after the last item (lane 1's U5 review, M1) | a file it did not ask for |
| a count whose records end early: `end`, another item's `set` or `list` line, or the end of the stream, before a set's last member or the list's last `path` line (lane 1's U5 review, M1); `end` before the last item; or the end of the stream before `end` | not complete |
| a body cut short by the end of the stream | not complete |
| a byte after a body that is not `\n` | a byte count that does not match |
| a body whose sha256 is not its record's `<sha256>` | a digest that does not match |
| items that disagree (13.4): one path named with two digests; where the answer holds the list, a `file` record's or a set member's path that the list lacks, or a set whose members are not the list's paths under its prefix; or an `absent`, `ambiguous` or `file` record that the list's paths, or those of a set that covers its name, contradict (the Codex review, BLOCKING 3); or, with a list or a set or without one, two such records whose names match the same paths that are not the same kind, or two `file` records among them that name different paths or digests (the Codex re-review, B4); or two lists, or two sets of one prefix, that name different paths (13.4, new in draft 19) (lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 19, a NIT; lane 1's U5 NIT on draft 19, #165 6887632287; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 20, a NIT) | a mismatch |
| a decimal field of more than 18 digits: `<n>`, `<size>`, a `<count>`, `<readers_from>` or `<version>` (13.4; the Codex re-review, S4) | a bad header |
| any byte after `end\n` | a malformed answer |
| more than 16,777,216 bytes, or a `<size>` that would carry the answer past them | an answer over 16 MB |
| an answer not whole, to the end of its stream, within the client's wait: 300 ms, or SVC-6's timer plus 100 ms (section 6.1) | not complete within the 300 ms share |

- **What the client does at each** (Y:4079; Y:4153–4156): it discards all of the answer, reads directly, and writes `.direct`, as for an `unavailable` line. It writes no cache file from an answer until the whole answer has passed every check above (proposal, lane 5). Reason: a fault found at the last record must leave no file written from the first, so that Y:4079's "discards all of it" holds on disk too.
- **Before any byte,** section 5.3's other cases are `unavailable` as well: no socket, a dead one, a failed directory or length check, an owner file of another namespace or boot, an owner file the client cannot use (section 2.4), and a server of another uid. A connection the service closes past its cap is the first row's, no byte before the end of the stream (section 7).

### 13.8 The client's read, in order (proposal, lane 5)

1. Check the path's length and the directory (sections 2.1 and 2.2), and nothing more: no owner file is read before the connect (the Codex re-review, B1).
2. Connect. `ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED` is unavailable, and the client starts a service under section 8.1's rule, whatever any owner file says; any other failure of the connect is unavailable, and starts none (section 7). Then, on the socket that answered and before a byte is sent, read the owner file and check it (section 2.4): one the client cannot use, or one of another pid namespace or boot, is unavailable, and the client closes the socket and starts none (lane 3's rulings, #165 6746655077, OP-8, and #165 6879724890, point 1). An owner file from another install: where the uid check that follows passes, the client starts one (section 8.1), and still sends its request (lane 1's U5 NIT-n, #165 6883749855), which the old service answers `unavailable restarting` where its own files changed (section 8.4's limit; OP-20), before it exits (Y:3915–3918; lane 1's U5 re-check, N-f, #165 6752336451). Then check the server's uid by `SO_PEERCRED`, or `LOCAL_PEERCRED` on macOS (section 3): a server of another uid is unavailable, and the client sends nothing and starts none, whatever the owner file said of the install, so the uid check wins over another install's start (lane 5's ruling on lane 1's U5 NIT-h, #165 6881986067; section 2.4). T512 reads in this order (`lanes-index`:9129–9180 at T512's `88b50886`, where a client kept another install's start for a server of another uid, :9177–9178; :9378–9439 at `26c77534`, where the uid check wins, by its commit `63f25fe9`).
3. Send the whole request at once.
4. Read until the end of the stream, the 16 MB bound, or the end of the wait, whichever comes first. The wait is one deadline on a monotonic clock, set once the request is built and before step 1's checks, so it covers the checks, the connect, the owner file's read and the uid check, the send and the read together (Y:4182; Y:4393; `lanes-index`:9867, before :9521–9531, at T512's `bb19b1ad`; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, SHOULD 2, #165 6885771975). Y:4393 times it from the `connect()` call; T512 starts it a little earlier, which only shortens it (lane 1's U5 NIT-s, #165 6886039744). It can therefore end before the service's timer does, and the client then reads directly (section 6.2). Under `--for guard` the deadline is the earlier of that start plus `w_s` and `T0 + 3.75 s` (:9868–9869), since an answer read whole past that line is too late anyway (Y:4185; section 6.1). That cap is T507's client half, under lane 1's R07 gate (lane 1's U5 review, N4).
5. Check the whole answer (13.4 to 13.7).
6. Only then write the cache family at `n`, `.head` last (CLI45:675), or, for a refusal, exit as the direct read would (section 5.2).

Reason: one deadline is Y's "connect and answer together" (Y:4182), and checking before writing keeps 13.7's rule that no cache file is written from an answer that fails.

### 13.9 Which forms ask the socket, and what each sends

- **Form 2, the guard's prefetch,** names all it reads: `file lanes/LANES.md`, `file lanes/aliases.tsv`, and `file lanes/log/<window>.md` where `--lane=<window>` is given and in the class (CLI46:666, CLI46:670). It never names a set or the list. So the guard's whole path fits the format.
- **Form 4** never asks the socket (B7; CLI45:747).
- **Form 1, the readers' prefetch** (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-5; CLI47:665; lane 3's ruling, #165 6747048305, ERR-31). One request names every member of `--files <set>`, or of `--all`, each by the render-set names it covers, on these lines (proposal, lane 5, for the lines, in lane 1's bytes, U5 M1):

  | Member | Its request line |
  |---|---|
  | `register` | `file lanes/LANES.md` |
  | `aliases` | `file lanes/aliases.tsv` |
  | `log:<key>` | `file lanes/log/<key>.md` |
  | `archive` | `set lanes/archive/` |
  | `paths` | `list` |
  | `--all`'s logs | `set lanes/log/`, which the client filters to the class (13.4) |

  - `--all` is `register`, `paths`, `archive`, `aliases` and the logs (CLI47:665), so it sends five lines.
  - One form-1 call reads one snapshot (Y:4890), so it never takes some members through the socket and others directly. The service answers the whole request, and an `unavailable` sends the whole call to the direct read.
- **Form 3, the readers' second read** (lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-7; CLI46:730–733, with CLI47:730 and CLI47:732; for its members and a log created since `.head`, lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, S-b, #165 6828035829, under lane 3's ruling, #165 6778890211, B19-cli:733 and B19-tasks:444). It asks the service first, as every reader's `register read` does (Y:3843–3845; Y:5443), and reads `.direct` as forms 1 and 2 do (C6). It rests on lane 3's OP-2 erratum, ERR-26, the generation in the answer (6746620707).
  - **What it sends:** one request naming every member the family holds, by the cache's own files: `.register`, `.paths`, `.archive`, `.aliases`, and `file lanes/log/<key>.md` for each `.log.<key>` whose key is in the class; a `--log-key=` pair, `.log.%<hex>`, is never a member (O18; B19-cli:729; lane 1's U5 review of draft 9, NIT-a, #165 6833882556). `.head` holds rows and names no member (B19-cli:694–701; B19-tasks:444). With them go every member that `--files` and `--log-key=` name, as file names only with no `n` (CLI47:730; Y:4611; B7). Each goes on form 1's line for it, above, so `archive` goes as its set, `set lanes/archive/`, not file by file (lane 1's U5 review, M1); and a cached `log:<key>`, each `absent=<key>` row and each `--log-key=<key>` go as `file lanes/log/<key>.md` (proposal, lane 5, for the lines). Form 3 sends the one `list` line on every call, whether or not `.paths` is cached, since its answer gives every row's digest (below), as T113 reads its listing on every call (`lanes-index`:6086 at line R's merge30). It writes `.paths` from that answer only where `.paths` is a member, so a family with no `.paths` gains none, and a path created since `.head` that no row, set or key names is no change to it (13.2; 13.4; the Codex review, SHOULD 1).
    - **`--all`'s logs are no member.** A log created or removed counts through `.paths` only (B19-tasks:444), by `list` against the cached `.paths`. `set lanes/archive/` stays the test for `archive` (S-b).
    - **A digest for every row.** Every `.head` `file=` row needs a digest at `n′`, its text cached or not. A log that `--all` dropped from the class, or one of two that differ only by case (C11), keeps its row with no cached text (13.4; S-a), and a `file` line on an ambiguous name gives `ambiguous` (13.4). So form 3 takes every row's digest at `n′` from the list's answer, by path, as T113 takes them from its listing (`lanes-index`:7046–7074 at line R's merge30). It never sends `set lanes/log/` for digests. A `file` line's record decides only its key's text, and whether the key resolves, is `absent` or is `ambiguous` (lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772; lane 1's U5 review of draft 9, #165 6833882556).
    - **Its cost.** One digest per path: a `path` line is its path and a 64-hex digest (13.4), where draft 10's `set lanes/log/` cost every log's body, 5,182,523 bytes for 39 logs today. The service reads those digests as cheaply as T113's own snapshot does, in the request's one transaction (`lanes-index`:6086; lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772).
  - **How it compares:** the answer's head line gives `n′` and the generation, and its list gives every path's digest at `n′`. They take the place of the direct read's in the rules of CLI46:730–733, with CLI47:730's and CLI47:732's set rule. So form 3's `changed=` lines come from the answer's digests at `n′`, and no answer carries a journal entry; draft 2's journal-derived lines are gone (lane 1's U5 review, M1).
    - a `.head` `file=` row is changed where the list's digest at `n′` for its path differs from the row: another digest; a path in the list for a row with `0` and `-` (CLI47:699); or, for a row with `1`, a path the list lacks (`lanes-index`:7048–7050 at line R's merge30; lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772);
    - a path in `set lanes/archive/`'s answer and not among `.head`'s `file=` rows under `lanes/archive/`, or such a row whose path that answer lacks, was created or removed under `archive`, where `.archive` is cached (CLI47:730, CLI47:732; lane 1's U5 review, S3). This set-member test applies to `lanes/archive/` only, and form 3 sends no `set lanes/log/` (S-b);
    - a cached key whose `file` line is answered `ambiguous` adds to the changed paths only the list's paths that match it, by T113's one function, and that have no `.head` row: its new case variant. The old path's row is tested by its digest like any other, and the refetch drops the key, as the `ambiguous` bullet below says, and carries the old path's row at its digest at `n′` (`lanes-index`:7058–7060, :7068–7074 at line R's merge30; lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772);
    - where `.paths` is cached, a path in the list and not in the cached `.paths`, or in the cached `.paths` and not in the list, was created or removed; a log created or removed counts by this test only (B19-tasks:444);
    - an `absent=<key>` whose record is now a `file` record now resolves;
    - a `.head` that is missing or out of form, or whose `n=` is not the call's `--since`, or whose `generation=` is not the answer's head line's, rewrites the whole family, since what is cached cannot be dated (CLI46:733, which is B19-cli:734; `lanes-index`:7066–7067 at line R's merge106; the Codex review, BLOCKING 1). The answer's `n′` is never compared with `.head`'s `n=`: after any write it passes `.head`'s `n=`, and the digests at `n′` decide what changed (the rows above). The caller passes `.head`'s own `n=` as `--since` (`lanes-edit.sh`:7071–7078 at line R's merge106). So a call with `--since 10`, a `.head` at `n=10` of the answer's generation, and an answer at `n′` 11 whose list gives every row its cached digest and holds no path created or removed prints `n=11` and `refetched=0`.
  - **What it writes:** from the same answer, the members `--files` names, or the whole family, then `.head`, as CLI46:731–732 say. One request is one snapshot at `n′` (Y:4890), so the direct read and the service give the same lines and the same family (CLI47:730).
    - **A log created since `.head`**, where `.paths` is cached, refetches the family once (B19-cli:733; lane 3's ruling, #165 6778890211). The refetch rewrites `.paths` from the list. It writes again each `file=` row `.head` had for a log still present at `n′`, at that log's digest at `n′` from the list's answer, its text cached or not, and drops the row of a log gone at `n′` (`lanes-index`:7068–7074 at line R's merge30). This holds on every refetch, the refetch of a `.head` that cannot be dated (above) included. A cached `log:<key>` whose log is gone gets `absent=<key>` and empty files (`lanes-index`:6282–6284 at line R's merge30; 13.4). That is lane 1's U5 review of draft 9, S-c (#165 6833882556). The refetch adds no `file=` row, `.log.<key>` or `.logpath.<key>` for the new log, whose text comes from the first read that names `log:<key>` (B19-cli:733). The next call does not refetch for it, since the cached `.paths` now holds it (lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, S-b, #165 6828035829).
  - **An `ambiguous` record** (lane 1's U5 review, 1(a), in place of draft 3's proposal that form 3 discard the answer and read directly; narrowed by lane 1's U5 re-check, N-g, #165 6752336451, as lane 3 accepted it, #165 6752360829):
    - form 3 exits 69 only for a key the call was asked for, by `--files` or `--log-key=`, as C11 requires of it, and as the direct read would (CLI46:673; CLI411:673);
    - a cached member form 3 names only because the family holds it, and that now matches two logs, is dropped from the family: its `.log.<key>` and `.logpath.<key>` are removed, so the first read that asks for it ends 69 (CLI411:673);
    - lane 3's reason: a read should not fail on a member it never asked for, and the ambiguity still surfaces at the first read that does ask (#165 6752360829). Round B9 records it in `research.md`. It is DEC-30, a decision, not an erratum, since Y gives no rule for a cached member that turns ambiguous (lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, N-3, #165 6828035829).
    - Forms 1 and 2 write neither `.log.<key>` nor `.logpath.<key>` (13.4).
  - **A `--log-key=` key the request cannot carry** (lane 1's U5 review, M1): a key with any byte outside 0x21 to 0x7E, such as a space, a control byte or a byte above 0x7E, makes the whole call read directly (13.3); a key holding `/` or `,` goes on its `file` line. Reason: one call reads one snapshot (Y:4890), and the direct read takes any key (CLI46:669, CLI46:671). Like a request over the bound, it is the client's own decision, so it writes no `.direct`, by lane 1's N5's reason: the service is not at fault. Lane 1 takes this (lane 1's U5 re-check, #165 6752336451), and CLI411:688 says so: `.direct` is written by "the first fallback in the invocation; never a direct read the client chooses, for a request over the bound or a form-3 key the request cannot carry, which is no fallback".

## 14. Changes

### 14.1 Changes from draft 1

- **The status line:** draft 2. Draft 1 is `51fbd17b`.
- **Section 13, new:** the bytes on the socket, lane 5's proposal for lane 1's U5 review, with each of OP-3's items:
  - one request per connection;
  - the request: `read 1`, a `file <path>` line for each name, and `end`; ASCII; at most 65,536 bytes; awaited for at most the client's wait, `w_s`, from the accept;
  - the head line, `text <n> <generation>`;
  - each file's record, `file <path> <sha256> <size>`, then the body and one `\n`; `absent <path>` for a file absent at `n`;
  - the end, the line `end` and the close;
  - `refuse db <cause> <sqlstate>`, with `-` where there is no SQLSTATE, and the three `unavailable` lines;
  - 16 MB as every byte the client reads for one answer, 16·2^20 bytes;
  - each malformed case, under Y:4076–4079's words.
- **OP-2 and OP-3:** proposed in section 13. OP-2's question to lane 3, on Y:4064–4066, stays.
- **OP-6:** the one reading the format needs is proposed in section 13.4, with the `ambiguous` record, pending lane 1's answer. The point stays open.
- **OP-5 and OP-7:** the format stays neutral (section 13.9). OP-5 now says plainly that a set through the socket is more than file names: a spec question for lane 3 and Brett.
- **OP-4, OP-11, OP-13 and OP-15:** unchanged, but for one line each: lane 1 is answering them.
- **The basis** is B3 `a138b1b1`. Section 0 says that B3's `design.md` differs from Y's at five lines that neither draft cites, adds draft 4.5 as `CLI45:<n>`, the same lines as `CLI44:<n>`, and says where B3's `research.md`, `spec.md` and `tasks.md` differ from the files draft 1 read.
- **Pointers to section 13** in sections 1, 4, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3, 6.3 and 11.1. The header's line on what the contract fixes now names section 13's proposals as the one place it adds values.
- **Numbering:** the open points move from section 13 to section 15, and keep their numbers, OP-1 to OP-15.

  3. **The listing:** draft 3's request line `listing`, and its answer, a listing head line with a count and bare path lines, are now `list`, `list <count>` and `path <path>` lines (M1; 13.1, 13.3, 13.4, 13.6, 13.7 and 13.9).
  4. **`refuse db`:** draft 3's four fields with an optional fifth after `schema`, and draft 2's grammar of any `[a-z0-9-]` word, are now four fields for `unreachable`, `auth` and `switch-files`, and six for `schema`, `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>`, with any other word or count malformed (M2 and 1(b); 5.2, 13.1, 13.5 and 13.7).
  5. **The client's lines for `schema`:** draft 3's "until then the client prints CLI46:767's line" is now CLI47:767's cause line and the numbers' line after it (1(b); 5.2, 5.4 and 13.5).
  6. **An `ambiguous` record in form 3:** draft 3's "form 3 discards the answer and reads directly" is now "form 3 exits 69 at once" (1(a); 13.4 and 13.9).
  7. **Malformed rows:** a `set` line with another prefix, which draft 3 called "a bad header", and a listing line out of order or not a render-set path, which draft 3 called "a malformed answer", are now each "a file it did not ask for", the latter as a `path` line; and lane 1's rows for repeats, surplus records and counts that end early join them (M1; 13.7).
  8. **`<n>`:** draft 3's "no leading zero" is now `0` or no leading zero (N1; 13.4 and 13.7).
  9. **A reader's wait:** draft 3's "at most once a read process" is now "at most once a `register read`, and never again in an invocation once `.direct` is written" (N3; 6.1).
- **Lane 5's one new proposal,** for lane 1's U5 re-check: a form-3 call with a `--log-key=` key the request cannot carry reads directly by the client's own decision, and so writes no `.direct`, by the reason lane 1's N5 gives for a request over the bound: the service is not at fault (sections 11.3 and 13.9).

### 14.9 Changes from draft 9

- **The status line:** draft 10. Draft 9 is `64ad87c1`. Section 14 now holds nine lists.

  - **S-c, the rows a refetch carries** (13.4 and 13.9), which corrects lane 1's own words in 6828035829: a refetch writes again each `file=` row `.head` had for a log still present at `n′`, at that log's digest at `n′` from `set lanes/log/`'s answer, its text cached or not, and drops the row of a log gone at `n′` (`lanes-index`:7068–7074 at line R's merge30); this holds on every refetch, a `.head` of another `n` or generation included; and a cached `log:<key>` whose log is gone gets `absent=<key>` and empty files (`lanes-index`:6282–6284).   - **NIT-b, lane 1's answer on lane 5's doubt 2** (13.3 and 13.9): form 3 sends `set lanes/log/` whenever `.head` holds a `file=` row under `lanes/log/`, and takes from its answer only those rows' digests, never a text and never a member; a `file` line's record decides only its key's text, and whether the key resolves, is `absent` or is `ambiguous`. Section 13.3's size bullet names the set. The cost is recorded: after any `--all`, every form-3 call carries every log's body, today 39 logs and 5,182,523 bytes; past 16 MiB the call reads directly; T512 measures it, and if it costs the budget, lane 5 brings the question back. Draft 10 adds one clause to lane 1's words there: a `.head` with a row for even one log costs the same, since the set answers every log.

  2. **Form 3's rows in section 13.4:** draft 9's "no `file=` row but the rows `.head` already had" is now "but the rows of logs `.head` already had that are present at `n′`, at their digests at `n′`" (S-c).
  3. **When `set lanes/log/` goes:** draft 9's "it sends `set lanes/log/` for those rows, and takes from its answer only their digests" is now lane 1's two sentences (NIT-b; 13.9).
  4. **Form 3's log members:** draft 9's "for each `.log.<key>`" is now "for each `.log.<key>` whose key is in the class", with the `--log-key=` pair never a member (NIT-a; 13.9).
  5. **A lost `psql`:** draft 9's "the child restarts in the background, as after any failure" is now "the next request starts a new child in the background, as after any failure" (lane 3; section 7).
- **History kept:** section 14.8 and earlier, and every earlier line of OP-7 and OP-10, keep their words.

### 14.10 Changes from draft 10

- **The status line:** draft 11. Draft 10 is `f49e4c25`. Section 14 now holds ten lists.
- **The basis** (section 0): rows for lane 1's third way on draft 10's cost (#165 6834158772) and for lane 3's post on the task sets, whose last bullets answer on Y:3995 and wait on the `list` shape (#165 6834196887). "Whose rulings" names both posts.
- **Lane 5's decision, on lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772:** the `list` answer's shape is this contract's own, not Y's. Y's SVC-7 defines no listing (Y:4034–4067), ERR-31 rules on what a request may name, and lane 1's contract leaves "how a set and a listing are encoded" to this one (B19-cli:666). So no ruling of lane 3's is needed, and:
  - **the `list` answer gives each path's digest at `n`,** in the line `path <path> <sha256>`, its fields in a `file` record's order, the digest written as a `file` record's is; the client writes `.paths` from the paths only, and takes the digests on SVC-2's trust, since no body comes to check them against (13.1, 13.2, 13.4, 13.6 and 13.7; section 5.1);
  - **form 3 sends `list` on every call,** whether or not `.paths` is cached, and takes every `.head` `file=` row's digest at `n′` from its answer, by path, as T113 does from the listing it reads on every call (`lanes-index`:6086, :7046–7074 at line R's merge30); it never sends `set lanes/log/` for digests, and form 1's `--all` keeps `set lanes/log/` for its texts (13.3, 13.4 and 13.9);
  - **a row is changed** where the list's digest for its path differs from the row, or the list lacks a row's present path, or holds a path whose row is `0` and `-`;
  - **a cached key that turns ambiguous** adds to the changed paths only its new case variant, the list's unrowed paths that match it; the refetch drops the key and carries the old path's row at its digest at `n′`;
  - **S-c's refetch** takes each present log's digest from the list's answer, not from `set lanes/log/`'s;
  - **the cost** is one digest per path, where draft 10's `set lanes/log/` cost 5,182,523 bytes of bodies today; draft 10's measure-and-bring-back sentence goes with it.
- **Lane 3's word, #165 6834196887, on Y:3995** (section 7): a failed idle check starts a new child in the background at once, so that the next prompt's request finds it connected (Y:3995–4000). Draft 10's "lazily" reading stays for a child lost while serving, which the next request finds (Y:4014–4016), and section 7's start bullet and its bullet on a lost `psql` now say which is which.
- **Where draft 10's text changed:**
  1. **The list's line:** `path <path>` is now `path <path> <sha256>` (13.1, 13.4 and 13.7), and "The list carries paths and no text, so it takes neither a digest nor a body" is now "The list carries no text, so it takes no body" (13.4).
  2. **A digest for every row:** draft 10's "So form 3 sends `set lanes/log/` whenever `.head` holds a `file=` row under `lanes/log/`, and takes from its answer only the digests of those rows, never a text and never a member" is now "So form 3 takes every row's digest at `n′` from the list's answer, by path, as T113 takes them from its listing … It never sends `set lanes/log/` for digests" (13.9).
  3. **Its cost:** draft 10's 5,182,523 bytes of bodies, and its measure-and-bring-back sentence, are now one digest per path (13.9).
  4. **The row rule:** draft 10's list of records that leave a row with no record for its path is now the list's digest by path (13.9).
  5. **S-c's digest source:** "from `set lanes/log/`'s answer" is now "from the list's answer" (13.9); and section 13.4's form-3 sentence and section 13.3's size bullet no longer send `set lanes/log/`.
  6. **The idle check:** "a failure restarts `psql` in the background (Y:3993–3995)" is now lane 3's words, and "It restarts lazily after any failure" is now "after a failure met while serving, at the next request …, and at once after a failed idle check" (section 7).
  7. **The `.paths` test:** it now opens "where `.paths` is cached", since `list` now goes on every call, `.paths` cached or not (13.9).
- **History kept:** section 14.9 and earlier, and every earlier line of OP-3, OP-7 and OP-10, keep their words.
- **The open points** (section 15): OP-3, OP-7 and OP-10 gain a "Draft 11" line. None opens, none closes, and no point stays open.

Section 13.3's bullet on a request over the bound, its list of requests the service cannot take, and section 11.3's sentence now say the same, so lane 2's boundary test has one number.

So section 7 gains no clause for T512's reconnect on a changed configuration or for its 1 s between starts, and lane 5's code-side NIT round changes the comments.
- **The Codex review of opensoft/openRepoTools-spec#46 at `94590d11`, folded** (lane 3's routing, #165 6877006381). It cites draft 11's lines; before this fold, draft 12 kept draft 11's lines 1–60, and its lines 61–838 and 839 on stood 3 and 23 lines lower. Each finding, and where it is folded:
  - **BLOCKING 1, form 3's refetch trigger** (13.9): draft 11's line 601 rewrote the family wherever `.head` named "another `n` … than the answer's head line", so after any write every call refetched, and `refetched=0` was out of reach. `.head` is now dated by `--since` and the generation, as CLI46:733, which is B19-cli:734, and T113's code have it, and `n′` is never compared with `.head`'s `n=`. The review's case, `--since 10`, `.head` at 10, `n′` 11 and unchanged digests, prints `refetched=0`.
  - **BLOCKING 2, the render set** (13.3): draft 11's line 456 named only the two prefixes' paths as the render set. The line now names it whole, `lanes/LANES.md` and `lanes/aliases.tsv` among it (Y:258–259), and says the list holds every path of it present at `n`, so form 3 has a digest for those two files' rows.
  - **BLOCKING 3, one view of each path** (13.4, 13.7 and section 5.3): the `file` records, the sets and the list must agree on each path's digest and presence, and each set must be the list's paths under its prefix where the answer holds the list. An answer whose items disagree is Y's "a mismatch", so `unavailable`, with no cache file written. T512's client does not check it yet, so T512 gains these checks (`lanes-index`:9127–9297 at line R's merge106).
  - **BLOCKING 4, the owner file and the failure stamp** (sections 2.4 and 8.6; 13.8): each file's bytes, how it is written and how it is read, as T512 builds them, each with no version line, and what a file out of form gives. A stamp out of form is no stamp, which costs a start and never a wrong answer. An owner file the client cannot read is OP-17, open for lane 3's ruling, with T512's reading taken until then.
  - **BLOCKING 5, the error semantics** (sections 5.2, 5.3 and 13.5; OP-4): no request meets a connect, so `auth` and `unreachable` are never sent, and section 5.2 maps every failure a request meets to its answer: a lost child, a connection lost during the query and every failed statement are `unavailable`. OP-4 gains a "Draft 12" line, which answers the question its record left (draft 11's line 889).
  - **BLOCKING 6, a configuration change** (section 8.4; 5.3 and 13.5): the service looks on each request, retires the held child and starts a new one with the new settings before another answer, and answers `unavailable` until it connects; a change that ends it answers `unavailable restarting`. This is the reconnect that lane 1's NIT-e found in T512 (N-2, above). The rule now stands in section 8.4, on the review's finding, and T512's comments can cite it there.

The second, when the child starts, stands on lane 3's ruling (#165 6827356485, point 3; section 7). The third and fourth, the base's `register-cli.md`:731 and :733 and its `exit-codes.md`:226, are texts the follow-up has mended, and this contract already takes the mended side (13.9; section 8.6), citing B19-exit-codes:226 only for its 2.
  - **SHOULD 4, the service's own bound** (6.3 and 13.4): the service sends no answer over 16,777,216 bytes, and logs the fixed phrase, as T512 builds it. A bound on what it holds while it builds would change T512, so this contract does not state one.
  - **SHOULD 5, connections at once** (section 7): the backlog of 64, a thread for each connection, each bounded in time, and what a client the service does not accept meets, as T512 builds them. A cap on connections would change T512, so this contract does not state one.

  - **B1, the owner file's read order** (2.4 and 13.8): step 1 checks the path and the directory only; step 2 connects, then reads and checks the owner file, then the uid, before a byte is sent; a read that races the next service's `rename` and finds the file unusable is unavailable, with no start. T512 reads so at `88b50886`.
  - **B2, a configuration rollover** (8.4; OP-18): the re-read configuration and the child's end in one step, under the worker's lock, and a configuration epoch on each request, whose answer is sent only under its own epoch. T512 does not do this yet, so it is T512's follow-up (below). Which files are watched, and by what, is OP-18, open, with T512's reading taken until lane 3 rules.

"Out of form" now means outside the reader's grammar, so draft 12's "a stamp out of form is no stamp" holds as written.

  - **S3, the first connect's retry** (section 7): a connect that fails, the first included, waits for the next request; a failed idle check and a configuration change start one at once; and no connect starts within 1 s of the last. As built, at merge106 and at `88b50886` alike.
  - **S4, the decimal fields** (13.4 and 13.7): each 1 to 18 digits, and more is malformed. As built.
