### 14.14 Changes from draft 14

- **The status line:** draft 15. Draft 14 is `e87aa62d`, in three commits, `01e8b9d1`, `13868b70` and `e87aa62d`, the head of opensoft/openRepoTools-spec#46, which lane 1's U5 review passes with no MUST and no SHOULD (#165 6883749855). Section 14 now holds fourteen lists.
- **The basis** (section 0): rows for T512's round 4 at `bb19b1ad`, with how `f21ca4a7`'s lines read there; for lane 1's U5 pass of `f21ca4a7` and draft 14 (#165 6883749855); and for libpq's documentation of `sslkey`. "Whose rulings" names 6883749855 among lane 1's posts.
- **T512's round 4, as built at `bb19b1ad`,** where draft 14 said "T512 at … does not yet …":
  - **N5** (2.4): the next start sweeps a leftover temporary socket, under the lock, before its own bind (`a447ef6a`). Draft 14's "never removed" is corrected: at `f21ca4a7` a leftover lasted until a start drew its name, and that start, exiting 69, unlinked it.
  - **N8** (13.3): each distinct request line is planned once (`93e936ff`).
  - **The waiting request's word, and NIT-A** (8.4): a request that sees a change is counted, and its wait also ends once a `take_change` that began after it asked has finished, one that finds no change among them; a wait while the worker is busy is held to the request's own timer; and `unavailable restarting` is given only where `take_change` has recorded, when the wait ends, a change that ends the service, so any other stop during the wait is plain `unavailable` (`28777868`, `7ca56a40`). Limits (i) and (ii) are stated there. Draft 14 said that T512 at `f21ca4a7` did not yet give the word by the change, since any stop during the wait answered `unavailable restarting`.
  - **NIT-B, the lock race** (8.3): five attempts at `register.lock` over 100 ms (`bb19b1ad`), with a stamp's write that holds the lock past them accepted as a limit.
- **Lane 1's U5 NITs on draft 14** (#165 6883749855):
  - **NIT-k:** the three gaps lane 1 found named with no rule beside them, the lock race (draft 14's line 354), N5 (line 154) and N8 (line 540), are now T512's round 4 as built, each with its rule, and the lock race with its limit (above). Lane 1 found the form safe where the rule stood beside the gap (its :365, :370 and :394); there too, as lane 5's brief asks, the gap is now built or accepted: the waiting request's word is round 4 as built, with limits (i) and (ii) (8.4, draft 14's line 371); N9's exception, a stamp written without the lock where `register.lock` cannot be opened, accepted as a limit, with lane 5's reason, since the code's comment gives none but "as before" (8.6, draft 14's line 394); and the `engine:key` form, accepted as a limit (8.4, line 365; OP-18). No sentence of sections 1 to 13 now says what T512 "does not yet" do.
  - **NIT-l** (8.4; OP-18): the service does not tell an `sslkey=` in engine form from a path; it watches the value as a path, whose snapshot is `absent` every time. libpq's documentation gives the form, a colon-separated engine name and an engine-specific key identifier, but no test by which libpq tells the two apart, so the test lane 1 recalls, a `:` in the value where OpenSSL has engine support, is named as lane 1's recollection.
  - **NIT-m** (8.4): B2's bullet says that an answer is "given", where draft 14 said "sent", since round 3b's check is made where the answer is given to the request, and the send to the client comes after it (`lanes-index`:8780 at `bb19b1ad`).

### 14.16 Changes from draft 16

- **The status line:** draft 17. Draft 16 is `94a18adf`, the head of opensoft/openRepoTools-spec#46 that lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16 read. Section 14 now holds sixteen lists.
- **The basis** (section 0): rows for the review, `spec-46-94a18adf.md`, and for lane 3's routing of it, #165 6885771975. "Whose rulings" names the review among the reviews lane 3 ran and routed, and its routing as no ruling.
- **Lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, folded** (lane 3's routing, #165 6885771975). It finds no BLOCKING finding, and cites draft 16's lines. Each finding lane 3 routes, and where it is folded:
  - **SHOULD 1, the word for a change that ends the service** (5.2 and 5.3, draft 16's lines 231 and 247): each now gives `unavailable restarting` for that change within section 8.4's two limits, where the request is answered plain `unavailable`, as 13.5 already did since draft 16.
  - **SHOULD 2, the order of the timers** (6.1, 6.2, 8.4 and 13.8; draft 16's lines 278, 286, 376 and 674): lane 5 chooses to state the order rather than change the protocol. The client's deadline is set once the request is built, before its checks of the directory and the path's length and its `connect()` (`lanes-index`:9867, :9521–9531 at T512's `bb19b1ad`); the service's timer starts at the request's arrival, once the whole request is read (:8985–8990; and :9061–9072, for a wait on a change). So the service's timer, 100 ms shorter, normally ends first, but the client's wait can end first, and the client then reads directly, the fallback, which is safe. Section 6.2's "Inside the client's wait" is now "100 ms shorter than the client's wait", with that order; 8.4's busy-worker wait no longer "ends inside the client's wait"; 13.8's deadline, and section 6.1's under `--for guard`, are no longer "set at the `connect()` call". The review cited draft 16's lines 286, 376 and 674 and 13.8; section 6.1's line 278 says the same of the guard's deadline, and is corrected with them. Draft 17 corrects those four passages only: section 13's line on lane 1's frame and 13.4's send deadline still put the deadline at `connect()`, and section 13.3 keeps Y's "from the `connect()` call" unmarked, which draft 18 corrects (Codex's delta re-review of draft 17, SHOULD 2).
  - **SHOULD 3, OP-18's values that are not files** (8.4 and OP-18's "Closed" line; draft 16's lines 370 and 1357): the one exception is `sslrootcert=system`, that value exactly, beside a parameter the URL leaves out or leaves empty; every other value of `sslrootcert`, `sslcert` and `sslkey` is watched literally, as a path, as `pg_url_env` gives it to the child as `PGSSLROOTCERT`, `PGSSLCERT` or `PGSSLKEY`, an `sslkey=` in engine form included (`register_serve_watched`, `lanes-index`:8167–8173 at T512's `bb19b1ad`). OP-18 gains a "Draft 17" line.
  - **The fourth finding, a NIT, the stale socket** (2.5 and 8.3; draft 16's lines 172 and 360): Y has the starter unlink it and bind; as built, the starter binds and listens at a temporary name, writes the owner file, and renames that socket over the stale one, as 2.4's "How it is written" says (`lanes-index`:9263–9271 at T512's `bb19b1ad`).
  - **The fifth finding, a NIT, round 3's B2** (the status line and 14.13's "What the code does"; draft 16's lines 5 and 1056): round 3 built B2's one step and epoch, with its rule for a queued request, at `26c77534`, and round 3b finished B2 for a request already running, at `f21ca4a7`, where both said that round 3 builds all four.
  - **Not routed, and not in this draft:** the review's sixth finding, a NIT, a line count in a source row of section 0, which lane 3's routing leaves out.
- **For the comment-only round then proposed as T512's round 5** (lane 5's brief; this bullet records only those comment corrections, which the broader round 5 built at `1e1846e3`, with S1 to S4 and NITs 5 to 8, later included; draft 20, after lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 19, a NIT): the comment at `lanes-index`:9056–9058, at T512's `bb19b1ad`, says that the busy-worker wait "ends inside the client's wait", and the start's docstring at :9209–9212 says that the starter unlinks a stale socket. ### 14.18 Changes from draft 18

- **The status line:** draft 19. Draft 18 is `48aa6bb2`, the head of opensoft/openRepoTools-spec#46, which lane 1's U5 review passes with no MUST, no SHOULD and no NIT (#165 6887224050). Section 14 now holds eighteen lists.
- **The basis** (section 0): rows for T512's round 5 at `1e1846e3`, with how `bb19b1ad`'s lines read there, and for lane 1's U5 pass of draft 18 (#165 6887224050), each counted by `wc -l`. "Whose rulings" names 6887224050 among lane 1's posts.
- **T512's round 5, as built at `1e1846e3`,** where draft 18 said what T512 at `bb19b1ad` did not yet do, for round 5. Each place now says what round 5 builds, with its lines, and what `bb19b1ad` did:
  - **S1, the gate** (8.4's "In one step, under one epoch"; 5.2's epoch line; the status line's sentence on draft 14's code; 14.13's "What the code does"; commit `0fa293f9`): a request has seen a change from the moment it starts its comparison of the stamps; it raises its check under `cond` before it compares, and an answer waits while any check is open, and is given only where none is open, its epoch holds and no change waits. Draft 18's round 3b text put "has seen" at the setting of `reload_due`; the gate puts it at the start of the comparison, which is earlier, and is the reading Codex's review gives (Codex's review of T512 at `bb19b1ad`, SHOULD 1).
  - **S2** (5.2's timer line; 6.2's queued request; 13.5's `unavailable slow`; commit `ba1d4a4d`): a job still queued at its arrival plus its timer is removed and answered `unavailable slow`, with no `SIGINT`; a begun job's connection waits at most 100 ms more.
  - **S3 and NIT 6, the start's bind** (2.4's "How it is written", and 8.3's "Holding the lock"; commits `6cc950f8` and `30ee202f`): the sweep logs a leftover it cannot remove, and leaves it; the start draws another temporary name on `EADDRINUSE`, up to 8 names, then exits 69, as before; and it unlinks only a name this attempt bound.
  - **S4** (13.4's agreement checks; commit `0cf57988`): each distinct name, and each distinct set prefix, is checked once.
  - **NIT 5** (8.4's "A change that ends it"; commit `30ee202f`): `change_ends` stands for each take's own requests, `(taken, upto]`.
  - **NIT 8** (8.3's lock attempts; commit `30ee202f`): only contention is taken for the lock held, and `EINTR` is tried again.
  - **NIT 7 and `1e1846e3`** change only comments, a docstring and the `status` line, which now says that the next start "replaces" a stale socket; the contract claimed nothing of them.
- **Two rules new in draft 19,** which lane 5 names for lane 1's U5 and lane 3's review:
  - **13.4, repeated views agree:** two sets of one prefix in one answer that name different paths are a mismatch, as two lists that differ are, which T512's client already refused and this contract did not state; both rest on one view of each path, and a correct service never sends either.
  - **8.3, a lock that cannot be taken:** a `register.lock` that opens but cannot be taken for any reason but contention is logged, to standard error and `register.log`, and the start exits 69, as for one that cannot be opened, where a held lock is a live service's, and the start exits 0.
- **Where draft 18's text changed:**
  1. **The status line** (line 5): "draft 18" is now "draft 19"; draft 18 is named, after draft 17, which loses the list's "and"; draft 14's sentence on the code says that round 5's gate closes round 3b's gap, as built; and draft 19's summary is added.
  2. **"Whose rulings"** (section 0).
  3. **Section 2.4's "How it is written"** (S3, NIT 6, the bind).
  4. **Section 5.2's timer line and epoch line** (S2, S1).
  5. **Section 6.2's queued request** (S2).
  6. **Section 8.3's lock attempts** (NIT 8, the new rule) and **"Holding the lock"** (the bind).
  7. **Section 8.4's "A change that ends it"** (NIT 5) and **"In one step, under one epoch"** (the gate).
  8. **Section 13.4's agreement checks** (S4), beside its new sub-bullet.
  9. **Section 13.5's `unavailable slow`** (S2).
  10. **Section 14.13's "What the code does"** (S1), a line of draft 14's list that draft 18 qualified, now as built.
  11. **Section 15's "Still open" line.**

**Part 5: the service started by the launcher's path as invoked, which corrects part 4's launch** (lane 5's test, on Python 3.12.3; lane 3's ruling, "`:7761`: close it", #165 6894116246).

- **Why:** `__main__`'s `__file__` is absolute but not normalized, such as `…/a/link/../lanes-index`. `abspath` collapses it lexically to another launcher's file, or to none, and the un-normalized path opens the real file, since the kernel resolves `link/..` physically. So T512's commit 5, `4e343ea2`, which corrects its commit 4, `e2212236`, starts the service by `__file__` as Python gives it, made absolute by `os.path.join(os.getcwd(), __file__)` where it is not, and never normalized. That keeps any link in the service's own `__file__`, so its install check, by the real path on each request, notices a repointed link as before where the service was started through that link, and there part 4's repoint cost does not arise; a service started by its real path, as `lanes-edit.sh` starts every one, does not see it, as at `23cb3799` and before (part 7; OP-20, closed).
- **8.2's first bullet, 8.4's "An install change", 9's "What it does", 2.4's `install=` line's launch clause, OP-19's part-4 line and the status line** say so; **2.4's "Another install"** loses part 4's exception; and **part 4's list** above names the launch as part 5 corrects it.
- **The install identity** is unchanged: the launcher's real path, as T512's `23cb3799` builds it.
- **The status line and this section's opening** record spec#46's landing, merge commit `73792183` (#165 6894049336), and follow-up 1's PR, opensoft/openRepoTools-spec#62.
- **The open points** (section 15): none opens and none closes.

For lane 1, the U5 reviewer, and lane 3, unless a point names another lane. Each names its sources. Where the contract must take one reading to be whole, it says which, and the point stays open. ### OP-4. `refuse db`'s causes

- **Closed:** lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-4 (CLI46:653, CLI46:767), see §5.2 and §13.5. The causes are `unreachable`, `auth`, `switch-files` and `schema`; under `--for guard` the first stderr line on the 69 is `lanes-index: refuse db <cause> <SQLSTATE>`, with `-` where there is none; a rollback's declaration counts only on `switch-files`; and a cause the request itself did not see is answered `unavailable`, which needs no ruling.
- **Draft 4:** `schema` carries the two numbers, `refuse db schema - <readers_from> <version>`, and the client's next stderr line names them with Y:2338's remedy (lane 1's U5 review, M2 and 1(b), #165 6747151224; CLI47:767). Lane 3 confirms it (#165 6747313608), see §5.2 and §13.5.
- Y names the definitive failures (Y:4082–4085), but only two cause words, `auth` and `unreachable` (Y:3931–3932): none for the switch not `primary`, or a refused schema version.
- The client needs the switch's word to tell "the switch at `files`", since a rollback's declaration counts only where the read found the switch at `files` (Y:4449–4453).
- It also needs a line for the 69's first stderr line, "the cause the guard quotes" (CLI44:767), while the service sends no `psql` message text (Y:3797–3799).
- And the connect runs only in the background, and a request before it connects is `unavailable` (Y:4014–4016). So it is not said when "the connection refused" or "an authentication error" is "definitive in this request" (Y:4082–4084), rather than a past background attempt's outcome, which svc:23's "never answer from what it read before" may exclude.
- For lane 1 and lane 3.
- **Draft 12, the question above answered** (the Codex review, BLOCKING 5), see §5.2 and §13.5: never, in this service. No request meets a connect or a login, so `auth` and `unreachable` are never sent, and every connect failure is a past background attempt's outcome, answered `unavailable`, as lane 1's answer reads it (6746620707, OP-4). Section 5.2 maps every failure a request meets to its answer: `refuse db` only for the switch and the window, which the request's own transaction reads, and `unavailable` for a lost child, a connection lost during the query and any failed statement, so the direct read decides, with the same 69 or 2 (Y:4089–4091; `lanes-index`:8547–8593 at line R's merge106).

### OP-5. Sets the client cannot name

- **Closed:** lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-5 (CLI46:665), and lane 3's ruling on the spec question, #165 6747048305, see §4, §13.3 and §13.9. Lane 1's answer: each member goes in one request by the render-set names it covers, `archive` as the set `lanes/archive/`, `--all`'s logs as the set `lanes/log/`, which the client filters to the class, and `paths` as the render set's listing at `n`; a set names files only. Lane 3's ruling: a reader's request may name the files it wants singly or by the contract's member sets, or ask for the render set's listing at `n`. B5 records it as ERR-31, which reads Y:4036–4037's "The request names the files it wants" as "the files it wants, singly or by the contract's member sets, or the render set's listing at `n`", and still nothing else, citing Y:4036–4037 as lane 1 corrected it (#165 6747151224). Contract draft 4.7 states it (CLI47:665). The bytes are lane 1's (lane 1's U5 review, M1): `set lanes/archive/`, `set lanes/log/` and `list`.
- **Draft 3's record, the spec question, for lane 3 and Brett:** whether a reader's request may name a set or the listing, when Y:4036–4037 says the request names the files it wants "and nothing else" (Y:4611; B7). Lane 1 has asked lane 3 the same (#165 6746948770). If the answer is no, form 1's sets read directly (§13.9).
- The request names files (Y:4036–4037; svc:59). Form 1's `--all` (every log at `n`), its `archive` member (`lanes/archive/*`) and its `paths` member (the render set's paths present at `n`) are sets whose members the client cannot know before the read (CLI44:665, CLI44:680–681).
- How they are asked of the service, under "file names only", is not said.
- For lane 1 and lane 3.
- Draft 2's format stayed neutral on it (section 13.9), pending lane 1's answer. Said plainly then: asking the service for one of these sets is more than file names, so it is a spec question for lane 3 and Brett, not a matter of the format. Draft 3 keeps that question open, above.

### OP-6. A log named in another case

- **Closed:** lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-6 (CLI46:673), see §13.4 and §13.7. The service resolves the name by T113's one function, which `serve` reuses; the answer names the resolved path, marks the name absent, or marks two matches. Draft 2's choice between an `ambiguous` record and answering the whole request `unavailable` is closed in `ambiguous`'s favour.
- Form 2 asks for `lanes/log/<window name>.md` (Y:4142–4144). The direct read writes `.log.<key>` where the key "resolves case-insensitively to a `lanes/log/<File>.md` of the render set", with `.logpath.<key>` the path it resolved to, and neither where two logs differ only by case (CLI44:673, CLI44:683–684; Y:4957).
- Through the service the client must write the same two files, or test 10's byte-identity can break through `parse_log_stream`'s `unreadable:` warnings, which name `.logpath`'s path (Y:4957; svc:86).
- Whether the service resolves the name, and how its answer names the path it resolved to, is not said.
- For lane 1.
- Draft 2 proposed the one reading its format needs (section 13.4): the service resolves the name case-insensitively at `n`, the `file` record names the resolved path for `.logpath.<key>`, and `ambiguous` marks two logs that differ only by case. Lane 1's answer matches its `file`, `absent` and `ambiguous` records, with T113's one function in place of draft 2's ASCII-only fold.
- **Draft 4:** lane 1 decides that T113's function compares bytes and folds A–Z only, for every key, and takes the `ambiguous` record; form 3 exits 69 at once on it, and forms 1 and 2 write neither file (lane 1's U5 review, 1(a), #165 6747151224; §13.4, §13.9).
- **Draft 5:** form 3 exits 69 only for a key the call was asked for, by `--files` or `--log-key=`; a cached member that turns ambiguous is dropped from the family, and its `.log.<key>` and `.logpath.<key>` files are removed, so the first read that asks for it ends 69 (lane 1's U5 re-check, N-g, #165 6752336451, accepted by lane 3, #165 6752360829; draft 4.11, to come, at CLI410:673; §13.4, §13.9).
- **Draft 6:** contract draft 4.11 states it at CLI411:673 (#165 6752661856), and section 13.9 now takes its words for the drop rule (§14.5).

### OP-7. Form 3 and the socket

- **Closed:** lane 1's answer, #165 6746620707, OP-7 (CLI46:730), see §11.1 and §13.9. Form 3 asks the service first, reads `.direct` as forms 1 and 2 do, and finds the cached paths changed in `(n, n′]` by their digests at `n′`, against `.head`, a cached `.paths` and the `absent=` keys. It rests on lane 3's OP-2 erratum, ERR-26.
- **Draft 4:** form 3 also counts a path created or removed under a cached set member, and `.head` has a `file=` row for every path read at `n`, present or not (CLI47:699, CLI47:703, CLI47:730, CLI47:732; lane 1's U5 review, S3). Its request names a cached set member as its set, and a key with a byte outside 0x21–0x7E makes it read directly (lane 1's U5 review, M1). See §13.9.
- **Draft 5:** a cached set member has one `file=` row for every path its set's answer names, its text cached or not, so a log the client drops is not found created at every call (lane 1's U5 re-check, S-a, #165 6752336451; draft 4.11, to come, at CLI410:703; §13.4). Form 3 exits 69 only for a key it was asked for, and drops a cached member that turns ambiguous (N-g, as lane 3 accepted it, #165 6752360829; §13.9). A key the request cannot carry writes no `.direct`, which lane 1 takes (#165 6752336451; §11.3).
- **Draft 6:** contract draft 4.11 states S-a at CLI411:703, N-g at CLI411:673 and the no-`.direct` rule at CLI411:688 (#165 6752661856), and sections 11.3, 13.4 and 13.9 cite them (§14.5).
- **Draft 9:** form 3 names its members by the cache's own files, `.register`, `.paths`, `.archive`, `.aliases` and each `.log.<key>`, since `.head` holds rows and names no member; `--all`'s logs are no member, and a log created or removed counts through `.paths` only; the set-member test applies to `lanes/archive/` only; `set lanes/log/` is the digest source of the rows whose text is not cached, never a cached set member; and a log created since `.head` refetches the family once, gains no row and no file, and is not refetched for again (lane 1's U5 review of draft 8, S-b, #165 6828035829; lane 3's ruling, #165 6778890211; B19-cli:733; B19-tasks:444; §13.4, §13.9, §14.8).
- **Draft 10:** a refetch writes again each `file=` row `.head` had for a log still present at `n′`, at its digest at `n′`, its text cached or not, and drops the row of a log gone at `n′`, on every refetch; a cached `log:<key>` whose log is gone gets `absent=<key>` and empty files; form 3 sends `set lanes/log/` whenever `.head` holds a row under `lanes/log/`, and takes only those rows' digests from it, at the cost section 13.9 records; and a `--log-key=` pair, `.log.%<hex>`, is never a member (lane 1's U5 review of draft 9, S-c, NIT-a and NIT-b, #165 6833882556; `lanes-index`:7068–7074 at line R's merge30; §13.3, §13.4, §13.9, §14.9).
- **Draft 11:** form 3 sends `list` on every call and takes every `.head` row's digest at `n′` from it, by path, as T113 does from its listing; it never sends `set lanes/log/` for digests; a cached key that turns ambiguous adds only its new case variant to the changed paths, and the refetch drops the key and carries the old path's row at its digest at `n′`; and the cost is one digest per path (lane 1's third way, #165 6834158772; `lanes-index`:7046–7074 at line R's merge30; §13.4, §13.9, §14.10).
- Form 3 reads at the head `n′`, and needs the paths changed in `(n, n′]` (CLI44:726–735; Y:4900). The request carries no `n` (svc:59; B7), so the service cannot answer "changed since `n`".
- Not said: whether form 3 asks the socket, comparing the answer's digests with `.head`'s `file=` rows for every cached member, or always reads directly, as form 4 does. Y:4900's "(SVC-8's rule)" points at Y:4157–4161, which ERR-04 reads as "always direct" for the guard's read only.
- For lane 1 and lane 3.
- Draft 2's format stayed neutral on it (section 13.9), pending lane 1's answer.

### OP-8. An owner file from another namespace: start one or not

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6746655077, OP-8, which corrects 6746215510's OP-8 line and answers lane 1's question on it (6746620707), see §2.4, §8.1 and §13.8. SVC-2 governs: an owner file from another pid namespace or another boot is unavailable, and the client reads directly and starts none; `ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED` still starts one, whatever the owner file says. In B4: an erratum, ERR-27, drops "another namespace or" from Y:3882–3883's start list, which keeps the install change that Y:3914–3918's `unavailable restarting` covers; and tasks:1393's "as absent" (B3's `tasks.md:1407`) becomes "as unavailable, and starts none".
- SVC-2: "each container's client refuses the other's service and starts none" (Y:3786–3788).
- SVC-4 item 1: any client that finds "an owner file from another namespace or another install" starts one (Y:3881–3883). T512: such a client "treats the service as absent" (tasks:1393).
- This contract fixes only that such a service is unavailable to the client (section 2.4). Where the lock is shared, a start there finds `register.lock` held, and exits 0 (section 8.3).
- For lane 3.

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, OP-9, see §2.2, §8.6 and §9. `dir-check` is never stamped: `serve` exits 2 naming the directory, and `lanes-index status` reports the failure itself, with an erratum on Y:3927–3929, ERR-28. Lane 1 reviews the `status` line (R44).

### OP-10. No `psql`

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6754633102, on lane 2's view, #165 6754620468, see §8.6. With no `psql` at start, or no usable one, `serve` stamps `no-psql` and exits 2 before it listens, so it never answers, `refuse` included (Y:3926–3931); the test plan's "answers `refuse`" (tp:2028–2030) gives way to it, by lane 3's erratum, which round B9 records. The older-`psql` half was ruled (a) in draft 7 (lane 3's ruling, #165 6754338646). 007 adds no case for a `psql` lost after the start (6754633102).
- Y: `serve` stamps `no-psql`, and exits before it listens (Y:3926–3931).
- tp:2028–2030: with no `psql` on its `PATH`, the service "answers `refuse`".
- This contract follows Y (section 8.6).
- For lane 2, whose test it is, and lane 3.
- Lane 5 proposed exit 2 for `serve` on `no-psql` (#165 6746631207), as for `path-too-long`, the directory check and `config-refused`: each is an end before the socket listens (Y:3926–3929). Lane 1's T102 follow-up gives the `no-psql` stamp 2 in `exit-codes.md`, and leaves this point's question open (#165 6746948770): whether the service ever answers `refuse` without `psql` (tp:2028–2030).
- **Draft 7, the older-`psql` half, ruled (a):** lane 3's ruling, #165 6754338646, on lane 5's question (#165 6754249002), see §8.6.
  - `serve` checks `psql --version` once, at start, before it listens. Where the version is older than 15, it stamps `no-psql` and exits 2, as it does with no `psql`. The request's statements are joined with psql's `\;` (§5.1; Y:3985–3988), and that join needs `psql` 15 or later (CLI411:234).
  - `no-psql` then reads "no usable `psql`", and Y's fixed cause list stays as it is (Y:3926–3931). Lane 3 rejects a new cause word, such as `old-psql`, which would change that list. It also rejects having no check, under which every request would fail late and the service's `unavailable` would hide the cause.
  - What the operator sees: the client reads directly, as after any stamp, and that direct read's own check refuses with 69, naming the version, with nothing sent (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752697506; CLI411:234). So the version is still shown.
  - It is lane 3's assembly reading, DEC-31, "a decision, not an erratum", and round B9 records it in `research.md`, beside ERR-44's erratum. Lane 2's T237 holds the case that checks it (§8.6).
- For lanes 2 and 3, on OP-10's other question only: whether the service ever answers `refuse` without `psql` (tp:2028–2030).
- **Draft 8, the other question, ruled:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6754633102, on lane 2's view, #165 6754620468, see §8.6. OP-10's open half goes as lane 2 leans.
  - With no `psql` at start, `serve` stamps `no-psql` and exits 2 before it listens, so it never answers. Y:3926–3931 names `no-psql` among the causes that make `serve` exit before it listens, and lane 3's (a) reads it as "no usable `psql`" (#165 6754338646).
  - The test plan's "answers `refuse`" (tp:2028–2030) gives way to Y:3926–3931, by lane 3's erratum to those lines, ERR-45, in round B9's part 2: "with no `psql` on its `PATH`, `serve` stamps `no-psql` and exits 2 before it listens, and the client reads directly". It is lane 3's assembly reading, and changes no SHALL.
  - Lane 2's T237 test for tp:2028–2030 keeps its first half and changes its no-`psql` half, as §8.6 says.
  - 007 adds no case for a `psql` lost after the start (6754633102).
- **Draft 9, lane 1's U5 review of draft 8** (#165 6828035829, N-3, N-4, N-5 and N-7), see §7 and §8.6:
  - B19's `research.md` numbers the readings: DEC-31 for the older `psql`, "a decision, not an erratum", ERR-44 beside it, and ERR-45 for the test plan's no-`psql` oracle;
  - the 2 is `exit-codes.md`'s `no-psql` row (B19-exit-codes:226), from lane 1's T102 follow-up (#165 6746948770);
  - `serve` checks the version by T106's version check, with the same parser and the same floor of 15 (CLI411:234);
  - a `psql` lost after the start is SVC-6's failure: no stamp, a restart in the background, and `unavailable` until it connects, with no 007 test (Y:3971–3973; ERR-45).
- **Draft 10, lane 3's answer on N-7** (#165 6833590985), see §7: the reading is right, in Y's timing: the next request starts a new child in the background, as after any failure (Y:3971–3973; Y:4014–4016), since Y's "lazily" means at the next request, not at once. 007 has no test for it (6754633102).
- **Draft 11, lane 3's word on Y:3995** (#165 6834196887), see §7: a failed idle check starts a new child in the background at once, so that the next prompt's request finds it connected (Y:3995–4000); "lazily" governs a child lost while serving, which the next request finds (Y:4014–4016).

### OP-11. The fallback line where the direct read fails

### OP-13. `serve --start`'s exits, and its first step

No socket try of its own; the client's start path; 0 when it launched `serve` or the rules start none; 2 for a refused configuration (Q6(c) included), a mode other than `primary`, or a failed path or directory check; 64 for usage; no stamp of its own. Its OP-8 clause is read under lane 3's later correction, and OP-16 asks lane 1 to confirm that reading.

### OP-15. The background whole-file fetch

- **Closed:** lane 1's part, #165 6746620707, OP-15, and its bounds, lane 3's ruling, #165 6746655077, OP-15, see §7. One connection: the fetch runs on the held connection, in its own transaction, between requests; a request that arrives during it is answered `unavailable` at once; a request's `SIGINT` never cancels it. Its bounds are `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '1000ms'` and `statement_timeout = '10s'`, the renderer's row (Y:3527), with an 11 s timer; on expiry the fetch is dropped, and the next miss retries. B4 records an erratum that adds a D9 row for the fetch: ERR-29.
- **Draft 4:** a request that expires while queued behind another request's query is answered `unavailable slow` with no `SIGINT` (lane 1's U5 review, S1, #165 6747151224; §6.2).
- **Draft 5:** the line above no longer names the fetch. A request that arrives during the fetch is answered `unavailable` at once (§5.3, §7), and the fetch runs between requests, so no request waits behind it (lane 1's U5 re-check, N-a, #165 6752336451).
- On a cache miss the service "fetches the file whole in the background, in a transaction of its own, never on a request's timer" (Y:4048–4050). A whole `lanes/LANES.md` took 1,219 ms at a 150 ms round trip (Y:4050–4053).
- The held connection takes one query at a time (Y:3982–3984), under a 180 ms `statement_timeout` (Y:4017–4020), and D9's table has no row for this fetch (Y:3516–3537).
- Not said: whether the fetch holds the one connection, so that requests behind it answer `unavailable slow`, or runs on a second; and its bounds.
- For lane 1 and lane 3.

### OP-16. `serve --start` and OP-8's case (new in draft 3)

- **Closed:** lane 1's confirmation, #165 6747475637, with lane 3's agreement, #165 6747456383, see §9. Draft 3's reading stands: `serve --start` makes no socket try, and runs the start a client runs on `ENOENT` (Y:4497–4499), so it never meets OP-8's start-none case, which applies only to a socket that answers; a stale owner file from before a reboot never stops the warm start. It exits 0 where it launched `serve`, or where a failure stamp younger than 60 s stands (Y:3934–3936). Lane 1 withdraws the clause of its answer's exit-0 line, "or lane 3's OP-8 case" (#165 6746620707, its :25), which rested on the OP-8 text lane 3 corrected.
- **Draft 5:** the withdrawn clause is named once, in the answer's posted words, "or lane 3's OP-8 case" (6746620707, its :25). Lane 1's U5 re-check says that both wordings name the same clause, which is withdrawn, so nothing depends on which is kept, and that the posted words are the more exact choice (#165 6752336451).
- Lane 1's position on OP-13 counts "lane 3's OP-8 case" among the rules under which `serve --start` starts none, with exit 0 (#165 6746620707). It was posted before lane 3's correction of OP-8 (#165 6746655077), and asked lane 3 what 6746215510's "another install" meant.
- Under the correction, `ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED` starts one whatever the owner file says, and only a socket that answers with an owner file from another namespace or boot starts none (§2.4).
- `serve --start` makes no socket try of its own, and runs the start a client runs on `ENOENT` (Y:4497–4499). So this contract reads it as never meeting OP-8's case: it launches `serve`, which exits 0 where another namespace's service holds a shared `register.lock` (§8.3), and binds where the owner file is stale, as after a container restart, which leaves its stale files on the overlay (Y:3910–3911), or a reboot. Its exit is 0 either way (§9).
- The other reading, that `serve --start` reads the owner file and starts none on another namespace or boot, would leave the `SessionStart` block's warm start undone after every reboot or container restart, until a client's `ECONNREFUSED` starts one.
- For lane 1, to confirm, and lane 3 if the correction meant otherwise.

### OP-17. An owner file the client cannot read (new in draft 12)

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6879724890, point 1, (b), see §2.4, §5.3, §8.1, §8.2, §8.4, §11.2, §11.3, §13.7 and §13.8. On a socket that answers, an owner file that is absent, unreadable or not UTF-8, or that lacks `install=`, or the client's own namespace or boot line, is unavailable: the client reads directly and starts nothing. Lane 3 takes lane 5's reason: a healthy service writes the whole file before its socket appears, so (b) costs only direct reads until a faulty service idles out, where under (a) a file with no `install=` line would start a `serve` on every read.
- Open, for lane 3. The Codex review asks for fail-safe outcomes where an owner file is missing, truncated, malformed or unreadable (BLOCKING 4). Section 2.4 now gives the file's bytes, how it is written and how it is read, and leaves this one case to this point.
- Y gives the file's facts and one rule: a client whose own pid namespace or boot id differs treats the service as unavailable, and starts none (Y:3782–3788). It gives no rule for a file the client cannot read. Trust in the service "rests on SVC-2: the checked directory, the owner file and the uid check at both ends" (Y:4074–4076).
- A service always writes the whole file, by `rename`, before its socket appears at the fixed path, and a service that cannot write it never listens (section 2.4). So on a socket that answers, a file the client cannot read is a fault or another hand's work, never a service starting.
- **The reading this contract takes until it is ruled,** T512's as built (`lanes-index`:9094–9100 at line R's merge106): a file that is absent, cannot be read, or whose first 4,096 bytes are not UTF-8 gives no fact, and the client makes none of the three comparisons, then checks the uid and sends its request; a file with no `pidns=` or no `boot=` line skips that comparison; and a file with no `install=` line reads as another install, so the client starts a service, which exits 0 at the held lock (section 8.3), and still sends its request.
- **(a) That reading stands.** It changes no code. Against it: a service whose owner file is lost answers the clients of every namespace and boot that share its directory, which is the case SVC-1's check is for (Y:3786–3788), and a file with no `install=` costs every read a Python start until that service stops.
- **(b) The fail-safe reading, lane 5's recommendation.** On a socket that answers, an owner file that is absent, cannot be read, whose first 4,096 bytes are not UTF-8, or that has no `install=` line, or, where the client has its own pid namespace or boot id, no line for it, is unavailable: the client reads directly and starts none. The direct read is always right, and the cost is direct reads until that service's idle stop, since such clients send it no request (section 8.4). It is one change to T512's client, at `lanes-index`:9094–9100.
- Either way, the file's bytes, its writing and its reading stand as section 2.4 gives them.
- **Draft 13:** section 2.4 states (b) as the rule, with what the client does: it closes the socket, starts none, writes `.direct`, reads directly, and under `--for guard` prints the fallback line once. It reads the file only after its connect succeeds (13.8; the Codex re-review, B1). T512 builds (b) at `88b50886` (`lanes-index`:9160–9173, its commit `690c2cc3`). The values in a file the client can use are OP-19.

### OP-18. Which files a configuration change watches, and by what (new in draft 13)

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6880543448, (b), see §8.4. The service watches the configuration, the reader password file, where `reader_passfile=` names one (lane 5's errata, item 2), and each of `sslrootcert`, `sslcert` and `sslkey` that the configuration's `reader_url=` names, each by its device, inode, size, mtime and ctime, from one `os.stat`, and not by its sha256, (c). Lane 3's reason: each of the five changes what a new connection does, and the ctime catches an edit that puts the mtime back; (c) catches nothing more, and hashing five files on every check is a cost the service need not pay. The details are lane 5's, as lane 3 left them (#165 6880934796): up to five files, since a TLS parameter the URL does not name, or names with an empty value, adds nothing, and nor does `sslrootcert=system`, that value exactly, while every other value is watched literally, as a path, an `sslkey=` in engine form included (draft 17); what libpq finds on its own, the default files under `~/.postgresql/`, the `PGSSL*` and other `PG*` variables and a service file, is not watched, a limit the contract states, and the variables and a service file never reach the child (lane 5's errata, item 3); a path resolves as libpq in the `psql` child resolves it, an absolute path as written and a relative one against the child's working directory, the URL's TLS paths with no `~` and no expansion, and `reader_passfile=` as `load_config` gives it, a leading `~/` expanded (draft 14's third part), and the set of paths is computed each time the configuration is read; a path's snapshot is `absent`, for `ENOENT` or `ENOTDIR`, `error <errno name>` for any other failure, or the identity `(st_dev, st_ino, st_size, st_mtime_ns, st_ctime_ns)`; any difference, a missing file that appears, a file that goes missing, a change of error or of the paths watched among them, is a configuration change, which starts B2's one step; it looks on each request, before the request is queued, as before; and what a change then does is unchanged.
- Open, for lane 3. The Codex re-review finds that a held child can keep stale settings where a file the URL names, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert` or `sslkey`, is replaced, or where the configuration or its password file is replaced with its mtime kept (B2). Section 8.4 states the rest of B2, the one step and the epoch, and leaves this to this point.
- Y: the service "re-reads `lanes-index.conf` and its password file when their mtime changes, and exits if the mode is no longer `primary`" (Y:3918–3920). No source names the URL's TLS files, or any test but the mtime.
- **The reading this contract takes until it is ruled,** T512's as built (`lanes-index`:8100–8121 at T512's `88b50886`): the two files, by their mtimes in whole nanoseconds (section 8.4).
- **(a) That reading stands.** It changes no code. Against it: a held child keeps a TLS session set up with replaced trust or client files until it reconnects, and a file replaced with its mtime kept, by `cp -p` or `rsync -t` for instance, goes unseen until its next mtime change. The re-review counts both as stale settings.
- **(b) Lane 5's recommendation.** The service watches five files: the configuration, the reader password file, and each of `sslrootcert`, `sslcert` and `sslkey` that the URL names; each by its identity, its device, inode, size, mtime and ctime, in nanoseconds, from one `os.stat`. A file replaced by `rename` has a new inode, and a file written in place has a new ctime, which no user can set back, so a kept mtime no longer hides a change. Any difference is a configuration change, as section 8.4 handles it. The cost is at most five `stat` calls a request. It is one change to T512, in `register_serve_config_stamp` and `register_serve_load` (`lanes-index`:8100–8121 at T512's `88b50886`), with the TLS paths taken from the URL's mapping.
- **(c) As (b), with each file's sha256 in place of its identity.** It catches nothing (b) misses, since every write moves the ctime, and it reads up to five files on every request.
- **Draft 14:** section 8.4 states (b) as the rule, with lane 5's details and their errata, and drops its open wording. T512 at `88b50886` did not build it, since it compared the two files' mtimes alone (`lanes-index`:8100–8121); T512's round 3 builds it, as built at `26c77534` (`register_serve_watched`, `register_serve_snapshot`, `register_serve_config_stamp` and `register_serve_load`, `lanes-index`:8145–8213; its commits `b9c34674` and `aea52c9f`). Round 3b's comment says that the password file watched is the path `load_config` expanded from a leading `~/` (:8160–8166 at `f21ca4a7`, its commit `f21ca4a7`). T512 at `f21ca4a7` does not yet tell an `sslkey=` in libpq's `engine:key` form from a path, which costs nothing (§8.4; for round 4).
- **Draft 15:** T512's round 4 leaves the `engine:key` form as it is, and the contract accepts it as a limit: the service watches the value as a path, which libpq does not read as a file there, so its snapshot is `absent` every time, and a change made inside the engine is not seen (§8.4; lane 1's U5 NIT-k and NIT-l, #165 6883749855).
- **Draft 16:** the snapshot is normally `absent`, not every time: where a file does exist at that path, it is watched, harmlessly, since libpq does not read it. libpq's test is checked in its source, `fe-secure-openssl.c`:1249–1262 at PostgreSQL's `REL_16_4`: where it is built with `USE_SSL_ENGINE`, a value with a `:` in it is an engine's name and key, but on Windows not where the `:` is the second character, and any other value is a file name, and it reads a key only where it has loaded a client certificate (`fe-secure-openssl.c`:1255; lane 1's U5 NIT-r, #165 6885968900, added by draft 18). It is no longer lane 1's recollection alone (§8.4; lane 1's U5 NIT-o, #165 6885309342).
- **Draft 17:** the "Closed" line and section 8.4 said that a value "that is not a file path, such as `sslrootcert=system`", adds nothing, while the `engine:key` form, not a file path either, is watched. The exception is now that one value, `sslrootcert=system`, exactly, beside a parameter the URL leaves out or leaves empty; every other value is watched literally, as a path, as T512 builds it (`register_serve_watched`, `lanes-index`:8167–8173 at T512's `bb19b1ad`; §8.4; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 16, SHOULD 3, #165 6885771975).

### OP-19. Values in an owner file the client can use (new in draft 13)

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6880543448, (b), see §2.4 and §5.3. On a socket that answers, an owner file is unusable where a known key, `pid`, `started`, `install`, `pidns` or `boot`, is on two lines, where its last line has no newline, or where its `install=` is not three decimals, which lane 3's ruling of (a) widens to "three or six runs of ASCII digits, one space between each, and nothing else" (#165 6894116246); the client reads directly and starts none, as under OP-17's (b). Lane 3's reason: the owner file decides where the client sends, so a torn or doubled file must not read as a good one. The details are lane 5's, as lane 3 left them (#165 6880934796): the client reads at most 4,097 bytes, and a file of more than 4,096 is unusable, so the bytes it reads are the whole file; its last byte, the physical file's, must be `\n`, so an empty file is unusable; a known key comes at most once, and an unknown key is passed over however often it appears; `install=`'s value is exactly `^[0-9]+ [0-9]+ [0-9]+$`, ASCII digits and spaces only, with no cap on the digits, or, with follow-up 1's part 2, which lane 3's ruling takes, six such runs, `[0-9]+ [0-9]+ [0-9]+(?: [0-9]+ [0-9]+ [0-9]+)?`, applied with `fullmatch` to the whole value (`_REGISTER_SERVE_INSTALL_RE`, `lanes-index`:9622 at T512's `4444ddc4`; the three-run form keeps lane 5's details' notation, as the record); the lines, their keys, `pid=`, `started=`, `pidns=` and `boot=` are read as before; unusable means OP-17 (b)'s outcome; and the writer's failure path stands: a service that cannot `stat` its own file writes `install=` with nothing after it, so every client reads directly and starts none, and the service stops at its idle stop, with no new exit code and no new `register.failed` cause.
- Open, for lane 3. The Codex re-review asks whether a key given twice, a malformed known value, or a last line cut by the 4,096-byte read makes the owner file unusable, and prefers the fail-safe answer (S1). Lane 3's ruling on OP-17 names an absent, unreadable or non-UTF-8 file and a missing `install=` or own namespace or boot line (#165 6879724890, point 1), and none of these.
- **The reading this contract takes until it is ruled,** T512's as built at `88b50886` (section 2.4; `lanes-index`:8997–9010 and :9160–9173): a key given twice takes its last value; a last line with no `\n` counts as a line; `pid=` and `started=` are not checked; and `install=`, `pidns=` and `boot=` are compared as text. So a malformed `pidns=` or `boot=` already fails its comparison, unavailable with no start, but a malformed `install=` reads as another install, and starts a `serve` on every read until that service stops, the cost lane 3 gave as its reason against OP-17's (a).
- **(a) That reading stands.** It changes no code.
- **(b) Lane 5's recommendation.** As OP-17's (b), the file is unusable where a known key, `pid`, `started`, `install`, `pidns` or `boot`, appears twice, where its last line does not end in `\n`, or where `install=` is not three decimal numbers with one space between. No other value needs a check: `pid=` and `started=` decide nothing for a client, and a malformed `pidns=` or `boot=` already fails its comparison. A service always writes the whole file, each line ending in `\n` (section 2.4), so only a fault or another hand meets this. It is one change to T512's client, in `register_serve_owner_read` and its caller (`lanes-index`:8997–9010 and :9160–9173 at T512's `88b50886`).
- **Draft 14:** section 2.4 states (b) as the rule, with lane 5's details, in place of T512's reading as built and of the 4,096-byte read, and section 5.3 lists the new unusable cases. T512 at `88b50886` did not build it (`lanes-index`:8997–9010 and :9160–9173); T512's round 3 builds it, as built at `26c77534` (`register_serve_owner_read`, `lanes-index`:9232–9259, and its caller's `install=` check, :9422–9424; its commit `595ccae4`), reads the file only as a regular file (lane 1's N6; :7625–7643, its commit `526c633f`), and pins the writer's failure path in its proof. Lane 5's errata cite rule 7's sentence in §8.4, and give the writer's value where its `stat` fails, `install=` with nothing after it (§2.4).
- **Follow-up 1, part 2, held until lane 3 rules on lane 5's choice (a)** (#165 6890303231; since ruled, #165 6894116246, as the part-4 line below records, and this line's words kept as the record of what was held): `install=` names both of T124's files, the launcher's three numbers and then, where `lanes_index_main.py` is beside the launcher's realpath, the module's three; with no module file it stays three; and where either file cannot be `stat`ed for another reason it is empty, unusable by rule 7. Rule 4 takes three runs or six, so that a three-run file read by a six-run client is another install, which starts a service, and the upgrade to T124's two files restarts the service by itself (§2.4 and §5.3; as built in T512's held `7dd91ccd`, `lanes-index`:8113–8148 and :9534). The "Closed" line keeps lane 3's ruling's words, "not three decimals", which lane 3's ruling of (a) would widen; if lane 3 does not rule (a), this part is not taken. Its landing order is part 3's, held with it (§2.4): `7dd91ccd` with or before T512's first release, and T124 only together with T512's `7dd91ccd` and `23cb3799` and lane 2's T237zzf, as lane 3's ruling gives it in part 4 (lane 1's R2 review of `7dd91ccd`, #165 6892234131; lane 3's ruling, #165 6894116246).
- **Follow-up 1, part 4: lane 3 rules (a)** (#165 6894116246): six numbers, with rule 4 accepting "three or six runs of ASCII digits, one space between each, and nothing else", so parts 2 and 3 are taken, and the question part 2 waited on is ruled. The landing order is lane 3's: "T124 lands only together with T512's `7dd91ccd` and `23cb3799` and lane 2's T237zzf, never with one of them alone", and `7dd91ccd` lands with or before T512's first release (§2.4). The upgrade to T124's two files restarts the service by itself where the files are replaced at the same real path, or where a link to the launcher is repointed and the service was started through that link, since the service is started by the launcher's path as invoked and checks its real path on each request (§8.2 and §8.4, as part 5 corrects part 4, and as T512's commit 5, `4e343ea2`, builds it; `:7761`, which the same ruling closes); a service started by its real path, as `lanes-edit.sh` starts every one, does not see a repointed link, a v1.3 limit (§8.4; OP-20, closed). The "Closed" line now carries the ruling's words beside its earlier ones.

### OP-20. A service of another install, running: a link repointed above the path a service was started by, a `link-estates` RELINK, a changed `OPENREPOTOOLS_BIN_DIR`, or a checkout run by hand (new in follow-up 1's part 7; widened in its parts 8 and 9)

- **Closed:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6894714082, option 1, see §8.4: "a v1.3 limit, stated with its consequence and its remedy in the contract, in `register_serve_launch`'s docstring and in the manual. The remedy: after an upgrade by repointing a link, stop the old service, whose `pid=` is in the owner file." Lane 3's reasons: nothing regressed, since `abspath` and `__file__` are the same for a real path, so the limit is the same at `23cb3799` and before; the remedy is one stop; and an upgrade in place is seen, as before. Option 3 is "a named follow-up after v1.3.0, in its own PR". Lane 1 is asked how the estate's installs are upgraded, so that the manual's remedy names that way; if v1.3.0's own install steps repoint a link, lane 3 rules again. And lane 3's ruling, #165 6895062857: "Option 1 covers lane 1's NIT 3, a second live install at another real path, as the same v1.3 limit", since "a hand-run checkout, a `link-estates` RELINK and a changed `OPENREPOTOOLS_BIN_DIR` all meet the repointed link's mechanism"; §8.4 states one limit that names those cases, with the same consequence, "A client of one install finds a service of another install running. Its own `serve` exits at the held `register.lock`, and the other service answers it with that install's code until that service stops for another cause.", and the same remedy, "stop the other install's service, whose `pid=` is in the owner file, or do not run two installs against one register at once"; option 3 stays the named follow-up after v1.3.0, and covers both cases; and, on lane 1's install answer, option 1 stands with no new ruling.
- Open, for lane 3, as lane 5's correction put it (#165 6894693645, on lane 5's reviewer's SHOULD-1, `R62-REPORT-1115.md`): a service sees a repointed link only where it was started through that link, and `lanes-edit.sh` starts every service by its real path (§8.4). How does v1.3 take this? Lane 5's correction also asked lane 1 how the estate's installs are upgraded, by repointing a link or in place.
- **(1) A v1.3 limit,** stated in the contract, the docstring and the manual, with its remedy: after an upgrade by repoint, stop the old service, whose `pid=` is in the owner file. Lane 5 recommended it for v1.3, since nothing regressed.
- **(2) `lanes_index_bin` keeps the directory as invoked,** so that the launch by `__file__` as given sees the link. It changes `lanes-edit.sh`, and helps only where `lanes-index` sits beside `lanes-edit.sh` as invoked.
- **(3) A client that finds another install reads directly,** and sends nothing to it; the old service then goes idle and stops by itself. The cost: direct reads until then, and the immediate restart lost in the in-place case. Lane 5 recommended it if installs are upgraded by repoint, as the smallest change that keeps every answer the new install's.
- **(4) The request carries the client's install,** and a service of another install answers `unavailable restarting` and exits. It changes the protocol (section 13), and during an upgrade old and new clients could make the services restart in turn.
- **Follow-up 1, part 7:** opens this point and closes it on lane 3's ruling, which came before the part was written; section 8.4 states the limit, its consequence and its remedy, and names option 3 as the follow-up after v1.3.0.
- **Follow-up 1, part 8:** lane 1's U5 confirmation of parts 1 to 7 (#165 6895008151) widens the point, by its NIT 3. The old service answers `unavailable restarting` and exits only where its own files changed, so any second live install at another real path, such as a checkout run by hand, meets the same while a service runs: its clients read another install, start a `serve` that exits at the held lock, and get the running service's answers (§2.4 and §8.4). Lane 1 finds that option 3 covers both cases. Lane 5 asked lane 3 whether option 1's ruling covers the second (#165 6895042328), and lane 3 rules that it does (#165 6895062857; the "Closed" line and the part-9 line). Lane 1's install answer, from the code at line R's merge166: an upgrade by `openRepoTools --install` replaces the files in place, and is seen; no v1.3.0 upgrade step repoints a link to `lanes-index`; and the remedy applies where `link-estates` prints "RELINK", or where `OPENREPOTOOLS_BIN_DIR` changes (§8.4). Lane 1's view: option 1 is right for v1.3.0; option 2 does not help, since `SCRIPT_DIR` as invoked would be `~/projects/xFactory`, which has no `lanes-index`; option 3 is the right follow-up; and option 4 is too heavy. Lane 1's NIT 1 also corrects lane 5's correction: its "from a linked working directory" holds only where the link is the launcher itself (§8.2).
- **Follow-up 1, part 9:** lane 3's ruling, #165 6895062857, on lane 5's question, #165 6895042328: option 1 covers the second live install as the same v1.3 limit. §8.4's one limit names the four cases, a hand-run checkout, a `link-estates` RELINK, a changed `OPENREPOTOOLS_BIN_DIR` and a link repointed above the path, with lane 3's consequence and remedy in its words; this point's heading names the same four; and the "Closed" line cites the ruling beside #165 6894714082. No point opens, and none stays open.
- **Follow-up 1, part 10:** `register_serve_launch`'s docstring now states the limit, as lane 3's rulings ask, at T512's `4444ddc4` (`lanes-index`:7776–7813), built by T512's commits 6, 8, 9 and 10 (`6a12ff7a`, `78876e98`, `d360d299` and `4444ddc4`): the same four cases as §8.4, and lane 3's remedy word for word; its consequence is in its own words, which agree with lane 3's. The manual's statement is lane 3's T341's.
